---
name: code-review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes — Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** — does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** — does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you — run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point — a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here — not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) — fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

On top of whatever the repo documents, the Standards axis always carries the **smell baseline** below — a fixed set of Fowler code smells (_Refactoring_, ch.3) that applies even when a repo documents nothing. Two rules bind it:

- **The repo overrides.** A documented repo standard always wins; where it endorses something the baseline would flag, suppress the smell.
- **Always a judgement call.** Each smell is a labelled heuristic ("possible Feature Envy"), never a hard violation — and, like any standard here, skip anything tooling already enforces.

Each smell reads *what it is* → *how to fix*; match it against the diff:

- **Mysterious Name** — a function, variable, or type whose name doesn't reveal what it does or holds. → rename it; if no honest name comes, the design's murky.
- **Duplicated Code** — the same logic shape appears in more than one hunk or file in the change. → extract the shared shape, call it from both.
- **Feature Envy** — a method that reaches into another object's data more than its own. → move the method onto the data it envies.
- **Data Clumps** — the same few fields or params keep travelling together (a type wanting to be born). → bundle them into one type, pass that.
- **Primitive Obsession** — a primitive or string standing in for a domain concept that deserves its own type. → give the concept its own small type.
- **Repeated Switches** — the same `switch`/`if`-cascade on the same type recurs across the change. → replace with polymorphism, or one map both sites share.
- **Shotgun Surgery** — one logical change forces scattered edits across many files in the diff. → gather what changes together into one module.
- **Divergent Change** — one file or module is edited for several unrelated reasons. → split so each module changes for one reason.
- **Speculative Generality** — abstraction, parameters, or hooks added for needs the spec doesn't have. → delete it; inline back until a real need shows.
- **Message Chains** — long `a.b().c().d()` navigation the caller shouldn't depend on. → hide the walk behind one method on the first object.
- **Middle Man** — a class or function that mostly just delegates onward. → cut it, call the real target direct.
- **Refused Bequest** — a subclass or implementer that ignores or overrides most of what it inherits. → drop the inheritance, use composition.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3, **plus the smell baseline from step 3** pasted in full — the sub-agent has no other access to it.
- The brief: "Report — per file/hunk where relevant — (a) every place the diff violates a documented standard: cite the standard (file + the rule); and (b) any baseline smell you spot: name it and quote the hunk. Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls — documented-standard breaches can be hard, but baseline smells are always judgement calls, and a documented repo standard overrides the baseline. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings — the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes — that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing → **Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions → **Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

    54	## 5. Amendment content (AMENDMENT.md, fixes before outcomes)
    55	
    56	1. Identity: freeze commit SHA recorded in RESULT.md; the amendment names the frozen v0 protocol hash, the confirm protocol hash, family list, seeds, salt, transferred `devPrior`/`minEffect`, sampling weights.
    57	2. No change to any threshold, minEffect, readout, proposer, scoring or bootstrap setting.
    58	3. One primary rule: `numericTrend`. Two primary comparisons on the test split (imputed multiclass Brier, per-family means, paired whole-family bootstrap difference
    59	   `numericTrend` minus comparator, protocol resamples and seed, 95% percentile interval): (A) vs `shuffledEvidence`, (B) vs `priorMajority`. Everything else (`v0Rules`, `fixedRule`, `shuffledEvidence`, `priorMajority`, always-none and any other) is descriptive and cannot
    60	   produce a POSITIVE/NEGATIVE claim: `v0Rules` and `fixedRule` choose from the operation kind alone, so shuffling evidence cannot change them and beating `priorMajority` would not show evidence use (review P1-3). `mechanismRule` is a dev oracle and is not evaluated on test. A single named rule with a conjunction of two
    61	   comparisons means no across-rule multiplicity correction is needed or claimed (P1-4). Secondary, descriptive: directional accuracy, coverage, invalid counts, trend-confound diagnostic, label agreement with fixture expectations, measurement-limited share.
    62	   Stated limitation (P2-5): the shuffled control for case i is another family's evidence from the cyclic donor map, so per-family differences in comparison (A) are mildly dependent and the unchanged independent-family bootstrap is nominal for (A); the amendment does not claim calibrated coverage for (A), and POSITIVE requires (B) as well, which has no donor coupling.
    63	4. Reading, applied mechanically, in this precedence:
    64	   a. UNINFORMATIVE (override) if any of: fewer than 10 test families; `meta.status != CONFIRM`; either primary interval is null or has a non-finite endpoint; the paired family sets of numericTrend and the comparator are not identical (pairing incomplete);
    65	      `numericTrend` has any invalid or missing prediction on the test split; `numericTrend` is degenerate (one predicted class on all of its answered test cases, or zero answered); or measurement-limited cases exceed 25% of test cases. The comparators `priorMajority` and
    66	      `shuffledEvidence` are exempt from the degenerate check (priorMajority is constant by design) but must have complete valid predictions, else UNINFORMATIVE. Degeneracy and the 25% share are evaluated from the ledger by the pure function, not by eye.
    67	   b. NEGATIVE iff some primary interval lies wholly above 0 (supported inferiority).
    68	   c. POSITIVE iff both primary intervals lie wholly below 0.
    69	   d. Otherwise UNINFORMATIVE, with the descriptive suffix "no improvement shown" when the point estimate vs `priorMajority` is >= 0, else "improvement not supported"; the suffix does not change the label (P2-7: a nonnegative point estimate alone is never NEGATIVE).
    70	5. Meaning: POSITIVE = on these fixtures numericTrend beat its shuffled control and the prior out of sample; not evidence for any model, production world or the naturalist feature. NEGATIVE = numericTrend is worse than a comparator with interval support.
    71	   UNINFORMATIVE = fixtures or counts could not decide; no claim. v0 exploratory numbers are not reused as evidence.
    72	6. Failed predictions are reported first and in full; no post-hoc relabelling, threshold change, family exclusion or reinterpretation. Deviations go in a dated addendum, never an edit of earlier text.

    89	- `confirm protocol differs from the frozen protocol only in identity fields`: deep-compare every other key; freeze hash file equals `protocolHash`; v0 `protocol.json` bytes unchanged; recipe tuples equal the v0 cycle (k) read from the file.
    90	- `split plan: 16 test families (12 original, 4 rising), no family in two splits, donors stay within split, assertNoLeak clean`.
    91	- `gate refuses`: unit tests of the extended gate with fake committed copies: amendment missing/differs, protocol differs, freeze differs, dirty closure, non-empty out dir, `--exploratory` with the confirm protocol; passes when all match.
    92	- `CLI honours --protocol`: a tiny throwaway 2-family protocol (seeds 9000+, never the confirm families) run through the smoke path produces exactly its families; v0 default behaviour (no flags) is unchanged (command parse check, no simulation of confirm families).
    93	- `reading`: positive / negative / uninformative on synthetic ledgers, each override (9 families, EXPLORATORY status, null interval, incomplete pairing, invalid predictions, degenerate, zero answered, >25% measurement-limited) and the precedence (an override beats NEGATIVE).
    94	- `negative fixture fools a naive metric`: a synthetic numericTrend that predicts at least two classes, has high raw directional accuracy on easy cases, yet loses Brier (overconfident wrong probabilities) to `priorMajority` with an interval wholly above 0: reading NEGATIVE while accuracy alone says positive.
    95	  Separate case: an always-`none` predictor is degenerate, so the reading is UNINFORMATIVE (override), not POSITIVE. These two expectations are intentionally different and not in conflict.
    96	- `non-negative point estimate is not NEGATIVE`: point +1e-6 with interval spanning 0 is UNINFORMATIVE ("no improvement shown").
    97	- `report lists failed predictions first and shows CONFIRM status and the reading table` (render with a fixture; assert order and no scripts/external URLs).
    98	- `delivered confirm outputs` (post-delivery group, section 8b only): reads committed `confirmation.json` and `ledger.jsonl`, asserts status CONFIRM, protocol hash equals the freeze hash, test families >= 10, `confirm.sha256` verifies, and the reading equals `confirmReading()` recomputed from the ledger.
    99	
   100	## 8. Acceptance gates (runnable)
   101	
   102	8a. Pre-run (before the freeze commit; the delivered-output test is skipped/absent here):
   103	```
   104	export PATH=~/.nvm/versions/node/v24.2.0/bin:$PATH
   105	pnpm install
   106	pnpm vitest run --maxWorkers=2 tools/test/prediction-*.test.ts   # `delivered confirm outputs` is skipIf(no committed confirmation.json)
   107	deno check tools/predict.ts tools/lib/prediction/confirm.ts tools/lib/prediction/output.ts tools/lib/prediction/report-html.ts   # root tsc excludes tools
   108	pnpm typecheck
   109	git diff origin/main --quiet -- experiments/prediction-envelope/protocol.json experiments/prediction-envelope/protocol.freeze.sha256 packages/ tools/lib/prediction/score.ts tools/lib/prediction/proposers.ts tools/lib/prediction/readouts.ts
   110	git commit ...                                   # amendment + freeze
   111	for f in docs/prediction-confirm-2026-11-02/AMENDMENT.md experiments/prediction-envelope/protocol.confirm.json experiments/prediction-envelope/protocol.confirm.freeze.sha256; do git show HEAD:$f | cmp - $f; done
   112	```
   113	8b. Run and delivery (after the commit):
   114	```
   115	deno run -A --no-lock tools/predict.ts run --stage confirm --protocol experiments/prediction-envelope/protocol.confirm.json --freeze experiments/prediction-envelope/protocol.confirm.freeze.sha256 --amendment docs/prediction-confirm-2026-11-02/AMENDMENT.md --out experiments/prediction-envelope/confirm-2026-11-02
   116	jq -e '.status=="CONFIRM"' experiments/prediction-envelope/confirm-2026-11-02/meta.json
   117	deno run -A --no-lock tools/predict.ts validate experiments/prediction-envelope/confirm-2026-11-02/predictions.jsonl
   118	deno run -A --no-lock tools/predict.ts confirm-analysis --out experiments/prediction-envelope/confirm-2026-11-02
   119	(cd experiments/prediction-envelope/confirm-2026-11-02 && shasum -a 256 -c canonical.sha256 && shasum -a 256 -c confirm.sha256)
   120	pnpm vitest run --maxWorkers=2 tools/test/prediction-*.test.ts     # now including `delivered confirm outputs`
   121	pnpm vitest run --maxWorkers=2                                     # full suite once at the end; a load-only timeout must pass alone, limits not loosened
   122	git diff origin/main --quiet -- packages/ experiments/prediction-envelope/protocol.json experiments/prediction-envelope/protocol.freeze.sha256 tools/lib/prediction/score.ts tools/lib/prediction/proposers.ts
   123	```
   124	Reproducibility: rerun into /tmp (never over the committed dir) and `diff` digests. Golden pins and RULE_VERSION untouched; no change to `generate`/`runFamily` means no dynamics change.

   126	## 9. Review
   127	
   128	Codex Sol 6.1 xhigh read-only on the amendment + code diff BEFORE the confirm run (amendment gaps are the main risk), and again on RESULT.md + notebook. Findings in REVIEW.md. Plan review: `review-sol-plan.txt`.
   129	
   130	## 10. Non-goals and limits
   131	
   132	No model or video proposer, no new mechanisms, menus, thresholds, readouts or retuning; no GPU; no claim about naturalist usefulness; no merging of confirm
   133	and v0 numbers into one table; no deploy, push or PR in this stage. One protocol, one run of record; an uninformative result is reported as such. Limits: 20 fixture families of 32x32 CPU worlds, one rising recipe, saturated worlds push labels toward
   134	"none", intervals are descriptive at this n, the shuffled-control contrast has donor-coupled dependence, label agreement with expectations is reported, never forced.
     1	# Amendment 3 (2026-11-02): held-out confirmation of the prediction envelope on fresh families
     2	
     3	Slice I02b, branch `claude/i02-confirm`. Dated amendment to `docs/prediction-envelope-2026-11-01/` (PLAN.md, EVIDENCE.md). It is committed, together with
     4	`experiments/prediction-envelope/protocol.confirm.json` and `protocol.confirm.freeze.sha256` and the code that runs them, BEFORE any
     5	outcome of a fresh family is computed. The confirm run refuses unless all three files are in HEAD byte-equal to the working copies, the
     6	executable closure (`tools packages experiments/prediction-envelope deno.json package.json tsconfig.json`) is clean, and the output
     7	directory is empty or absent. CPU only (sim-ref): no model call, no GPU, no network, no paid compute. This amendment is about the v0
     8	CPU fixture mechanisms only; it says nothing about any model, GPU world, production preset or the naturalist feature.
     9	
    10	Earlier text in this directory and in EVIDENCE.md is not edited. Where this amendment adds to it, it says so.
    11	
    12	## 1. Identity
    13	
    14	- Frozen v0 protocol (unchanged, not touched): `experiments/prediction-envelope/protocol.json`, hash
    15	  `ad924e6bf6caaf78f99e1785728657d5f4a02e2938fa9d5d847140230b8da3b0` (`protocol.freeze.sha256`).
    16	- Confirm protocol: `experiments/prediction-envelope/protocol.confirm.json`, hash
    17	  `07dbf7333dcfc6d150c51a1071ef4f9c9616fa9bf32beec5d1187ff256bf620b` (`protocol.confirm.freeze.sha256`). `protocolHash` covers the families,
    18	  so fresh families cannot live in the v0 file without changing its hash; the confirm protocol is a second file that is the frozen protocol
    19	  with ONLY `salt`, `families`, `cohorts`, `donors`, `nDev`, `nQualify` replaced. Every other key (world, window, menu, mechanisms,
    20	  proposers and their parameters, `minEffect`, `minEffectCandidates`, `devPrior`, `bootstrap`, `caps`, `seedVariantOffset`, versions, `frozen`)
    21	  is byte-equal to v0 (canonical form), as `tools/test/prediction-confirm.test.ts` checks. This is a new protocol file sharing v0's frozen
    22	  parameters, not "the v0 file unchanged".
    23	- Salt `i02-prediction-confirm-2026-11-02`. Family ids `c00..c19` (v0 used `f00..f31`). Seeds `6000..6019` (v0: `5000..5031`; v0 variants
    24	  `+7919` = `12919..12950`; the confirm protocol's would-be variants `13919..13938`; none overlap). The tests prove disjointness by effective
    25	  simulation identity, `(seed, light, nutrient, founders)` and seed plus variant offset, against v0 `protocol.json` AND v0 `recipes.jsonl`,
    26	  without simulating anything.
    27	- 20 families, from the pure function `freshFamilies()` (`tools/lib/prediction/confirm.ts`): 15 original-style (indices `k = 0..14` of the v0
    28	  cycle: `light = [spots, gradient][k % 2]`, `nutrient = [0, 1, 2][k % 3]`, `founders = 3 + k % 3`) and 5 in cohort `rising` (`spots`, nutrient 50,
    29	  founders `1..5`, a prefix of the v0 rising list). A test reads the v0 `protocol.json` and asserts each fresh tuple equals the v0 tuple at the
    30	  same cycle index. Rising share is 25% (5 of 20), equal to v0's overall rising share (8 of 32).
    31	- Splits, from the unchanged `assignSplits`/`splitPlanOf` (a pure hash ranking, no outcome involved): `nDev` 3 original plus 1 rising,
    32	  `nQualify` 0. Dev: `c03 c01 c07 c18`. Test (16 families = 12 original + 4 rising, 96 cases): `c05 c10 c11 c06 c08 c13 c00 c14 c04 c09 c02 c12 c19 c17 c16 c15`.
    33	  16 is at least `MIN_FAMILIES_FOR_INTERVALS` (10). Donors for the shuffled control are the frozen within-split derangement stored in the protocol file.
    34	- Transferred, not re-derived: `minEffect` 25 and `devPrior` `{up 135, down 271, none 594}` are taken from the v0 freeze unchanged. The fresh dev
    35	  families only satisfy the split machinery (the oracle `mechanismRule` runs on them, descriptively) and play no role in any decision. There is
    36	  no qualify subset and no dev stage, no threshold or prior is tuned on any fresh family.
    37	- Size and budget were fixed from v0 evidence (`v0/meta.json`: 327641 ms for 32 families plus variants on an M1 Max, so about 3.5 minutes idle for 20
    38	  families without variants and about 10 minutes at load x3). No smoke, timing, dev or generate run touched a fresh family before this commit.
    39	  The size is never changed after the freeze commit.
    40	- RESULT.md records the freeze commit SHA (the commit that contains this file).
    41	
    42	## 2. No change
    43	
    44	No threshold, `minEffect`, readout, proposer parameter, proposer, scoring rule, bootstrap setting (2000 resamples, seed 20261101), shuffled-control
    45	construction, or fixture is changed. `tools/lib/prediction/score.ts`, `proposers.ts`, `readouts.ts`, `fixtures.ts` and the generate/scoring path of
    46	`run.ts` are untouched. New code only adds the fresh-family recipe, the confirm gate and CLI plumbing, the ledger and the reading (all in
    47	`confirm.ts`, `predict.ts`, `output.ts`, `report-html.ts`).
    48	
    49	## 3. Primary comparisons
    50	
    51	One primary rule: `numericTrend`, the single evidence-using proposer nominated in advance. Two primary comparisons on the test split, both on
    52	imputed multiclass Brier (abstentions, invalid and missing predictions scored with the frozen dev prior), per-family means, paired whole-family
    53	bootstrap of the difference `numericTrend` minus comparator with the protocol's resamples and seed, 95% percentile interval:
    54	
    55	- (A) against `shuffledEvidence`, its own evidence-shuffled control;
    56	- (B) against `priorMajority`.
    57	
    58	Everything else (`v0Rules`, `fixedRule`, `shuffledEvidence`, `priorMajority`, `alwaysNone`, `abstainAll` and anything added later) is descriptive
    59	and cannot produce a POSITIVE or NEGATIVE claim: `v0Rules` and `fixedRule` choose from the operation kind alone, so shuffling evidence cannot
    60	change them and beating `priorMajority` would not show evidence use. `mechanismRule` is a dev oracle and is not evaluated on test. One named rule
    61	with a conjunction of two comparisons means no across-rule multiplicity correction is needed or claimed. Secondary, descriptive: directional
    62	accuracy, coverage, invalid counts, the trend-confound diagnostic, label agreement with fixture expectations, the measurement-limited share.
    63	
    64	Stated limitation: the shuffled control for case i is another family's evidence from the cyclic donor map, so per-family differences in (A) are
    65	mildly dependent and the independent-family bootstrap is nominal for (A); this amendment does not claim calibrated coverage for (A). POSITIVE
    66	requires (B) as well, which has no donor coupling.
    67	
    68	## 4. Reading, applied mechanically, in this precedence
    69	
    70	The reading is the pure function `confirmReading` on the committed `ledger.jsonl` (one row per test case per non-oracle proposer) and the
    71	parameters in `confirmation.json`; the delivered-output test recomputes it from the ledger.
    72	
    73	a. **UNINFORMATIVE (override)** if any of: fewer than 10 test families; `meta.json` status is not `CONFIRM`; either primary interval is null or
    74	   has a non-finite endpoint; the paired family or case sets of `numericTrend` and a comparator are not identical; `numericTrend` has any invalid
    75	   or missing prediction on the test split; `numericTrend` answers zero test cases or predicts one class on all its answered test cases
    76	   (degenerate); `priorMajority` or `shuffledEvidence` has any invalid or missing prediction (they are exempt from the degenerate check:
    77	   `priorMajority` is constant by design); measurement-limited cases exceed 25% of test cases. An override beats NEGATIVE and POSITIVE.
    78	b. **NEGATIVE** iff some primary interval lies wholly above 0 (supported inferiority of `numericTrend`).
    79	c. **POSITIVE** iff both primary intervals lie wholly below 0.
    80	d. Otherwise **UNINFORMATIVE**, with the descriptive suffix "no improvement shown" when the point estimate against `priorMajority` is at least 0,
    81	   else "improvement not supported". The suffix does not change the label; a nonnegative point estimate alone is never NEGATIVE.
    82	
    83	## 5. Meaning
    84	
    85	- POSITIVE: on these fixtures `numericTrend` beat its shuffled control and the prior out of sample. Not evidence for any model, production world
    86	  or the naturalist feature.
    87	- NEGATIVE: `numericTrend` is worse than a comparator with interval support.
    88	- UNINFORMATIVE: the fixtures or counts could not decide; no claim.
    89	- v0 exploratory numbers are not reused as evidence and are never merged into one table with these.
    90	
    91	## 6. Reporting and attempts
    92	
    93	- Failed predictions are reported first and in full (RESULT.md, `report.html`); no post-hoc relabelling, threshold change, family exclusion or
    94	  reinterpretation. Deviations go in a dated addendum below, never an edit of earlier text.
    95	- The first completed run is the attempt of record and stays committed. Replays for reproducibility go to `/tmp` only and are compared by digest.
    96	  If the run fails or a scoring bug is found: stop, record it in `docs/prediction-confirm-2026-11-02/BUG.md`, keep partial output untouched. A
    97	  rerun on changed code needs a new dated amendment AND a newly drawn disjoint family set (new salt and seed range), because these families
    98	  would have been seen. A crash before any outcome is written (for example out of memory) may be rerun unchanged on the same families.
    99	- `envelopes.jsonl` and `predictions.jsonl` are gitignored like v0, with digests in `canonical.sha256`; `confirm.sha256` seals `confirmation.json`
   100	  and `ledger.jsonl`.
   101	
   102	## Limits
   103	
   104	20 fixture families of 32x32 CPU worlds, one rising recipe; saturated worlds push labels toward "none"; the intervals are descriptive at this n;
   105	the shuffled-control contrast has donor-coupled dependence; label agreement with expectations is reported, never forced.

     1	// I02b: held-out confirmation of the prediction envelope on fresh families (docs/prediction-confirm-2026-11-02/AMENDMENT.md).
     2	// Pure: no file, clock or git access (tools/predict.ts does the I/O). Holds the fresh-family recipe, the confirm protocol builder
     3	// (the frozen v0 protocol with ONLY identity fields replaced), the disjointness helper, the extended committed-amendment gate, the
     4	// ledger of test-split scoring inputs, and the mechanical reading. Nothing here touches scoring, proposers, readouts or fixtures.
     5	import { canonical, sha256Text } from "../propose.ts";
     6	import type { Direction3 } from "./envelope.ts";
     7	import { labelOf, type FamilySpec } from "./fixtures.ts";
     8	import { buildProposers, freezeRefusal, measurementLimited, scoredCases, splitPlanOf, type CaseRow, type PredictionLine, type Protocol } from "./run.ts";
     9	import { MIN_FAMILIES_FOR_INTERVALS, brierOf, familyBootstrap, familyMeans, pairedFamilyDiff, type Interval } from "./score.ts";
    10	import { donorsFor } from "./splits.ts";

    14	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    15	// The fresh-family recipe (fixed in the amendment, before any outcome).
    16	
    17	export const CONFIRM_SALT = "i02-prediction-confirm-2026-11-02";
    18	export const CONFIRM_SEED0 = 6000;
    19	export const N_ORIGINAL = 15;
    20	export const N_RISING = 5;
    21	/** Keys that differ between protocol.confirm.json and the frozen v0 protocol.json. Every other key is byte-equal. */
    22	export const IDENTITY_KEYS = ["salt", "families", "cohorts", "donors", "nDev", "nQualify"] as const;
    23	
    24	/** 15 original-style families (the v0 cycle, indices 0..14) and 5 rising families (founders 1..5, a prefix of the v0 rising list). */
    25	export function freshFamilies(): FamilySpec[] {
    26	  const lights = ["spots", "gradient"] as const;
    27	  const nutrients = [0, 1, 2];
    28	  const id = (i: number): string => `c${String(i).padStart(2, "0")}`;
    29	  const fams: FamilySpec[] = Array.from({ length: N_ORIGINAL }, (_, k) => ({ id: id(k), seed: CONFIRM_SEED0 + k, light: lights[k % 2], nutrient: nutrients[k % 3], founders: 3 + (k % 3) }));
    30	  for (let j = 0; j < N_RISING; j++) fams.push({ id: id(N_ORIGINAL + j), seed: CONFIRM_SEED0 + N_ORIGINAL + j, light: "spots", nutrient: 50, founders: j + 1, cohort: "rising" });
    31	  return fams;
    32	}
    33	
    34	/** The confirm protocol: the frozen protocol with ONLY the identity fields replaced. Splits: 3+1 dev, 12+4 test, no qualify subset. */
    35	export function buildConfirmProtocol(v0: Protocol): Protocol {
    36	  if (!v0.frozen || v0.minEffect === null || v0.devPrior === null) throw new Error("the base protocol is not frozen");
    37	  const p: Protocol = structuredClone(v0);
    38	  p.salt = CONFIRM_SALT;
    39	  p.families = freshFamilies();
    40	  p.cohorts = [{ id: "rising", nDev: 1 }];
    41	  p.nDev = 3;
    42	  p.nQualify = 0;
    43	  p.donors = {};
    44	  p.donors = donorsFor(splitPlanOf(p), p.salt);
    45	  const problems = identityProblems(p, v0);
    46	  if (problems.length) throw new Error(`confirm protocol differs from v0 outside the identity fields: ${problems.join("; ")}`);
    47	  return p;
    48	}
    49	
    50	/** Keys outside IDENTITY_KEYS whose canonical form differs between the two protocols (empty when the confirm protocol is only an identity change). */
    51	export function identityProblems(confirm: Protocol, v0: Protocol): string[] {
    52	  const keys = new Set([...Object.keys(confirm), ...Object.keys(v0)]);
    53	  const out: string[] = [];
    54	  for (const k of keys) {
    55	    if ((IDENTITY_KEYS as readonly string[]).includes(k)) continue;
    56	    const a = (confirm as unknown as Record<string, unknown>)[k], b = (v0 as unknown as Record<string, unknown>)[k];
    57	    if ((a === undefined) !== (b === undefined) || (a !== undefined && canonical(a) !== canonical(b))) out.push(k);
    58	  }
    59	  return out;
    60	}
    61	
    62	/** The light/nutrient/founders tuple each fresh family must carry, read from the v0 protocol (never retyped): the v0 recipe at the same cycle index. */
    63	export function recipeTupleProblems(confirm: Protocol, v0: Protocol): string[] {
    64	  const out: string[] = [];
    65	  const v0Orig = v0.families.filter((f) => f.cohort === undefined);
    66	  const v0Rising = v0.families.filter((f) => f.cohort === "rising");
    67	  const orig = confirm.families.filter((f) => f.cohort === undefined);
    68	  const rising = confirm.families.filter((f) => f.cohort === "rising");
    69	  if (orig.length + rising.length !== confirm.families.length) out.push("a family names a cohort other than rising");
    70	  orig.forEach((f, k) => {
    71	    const m = v0Orig[k];
    72	    if (!m) out.push(`${f.id}: v0 has no original family at index ${k}`);
    73	    else if (f.light !== m.light || f.nutrient !== m.nutrient || f.founders !== m.founders) out.push(`${f.id}: tuple differs from v0 ${m.id}`);
    74	  });
    75	  rising.forEach((f, j) => {
    76	    const m = v0Rising[j];
    77	    if (!m) out.push(`${f.id}: v0 has no rising family at index ${j}`);
    78	    else if (f.light !== m.light || f.nutrient !== m.nutrient || f.founders !== m.founders) out.push(`${f.id}: tuple differs from v0 ${m.id}`);
    79	  });
    80	  return out;
    81	}
    82	
    83	export interface V0RecipeRow {
    84	  seed: number;
    85	  recipe: { family: { light: string; nutrient: number; founders: number; seed: number } };
    86	}
    87	/**
    88	 * Disjointness by effective simulation identity, without simulating anything: seeds alone, seed + seedVariantOffset, and the
    89	 * (seed, light, nutrient, founders) tuple, of the confirm protocol against v0's protocol.json AND v0's recipes.jsonl (variants included).
    90	 * History ids embed family ids and are not a valid disjointness key.
    91	 */
    92	export function disjointnessProblems(confirm: Protocol, v0: Protocol, v0Recipes: readonly V0RecipeRow[]): string[] {
    93	  const out: string[] = [];
    94	  const v0Seeds = new Set<number>();
    95	  const v0Tuples = new Set<string>();
    96	  const tuple = (s: number, l: string, n: number, f: number): string => `${s}|${l}|${n}|${f}`;
    97	  for (const f of v0.families) {
    98	    v0Seeds.add(f.seed);
    99	    v0Seeds.add(f.seed + v0.seedVariantOffset);
   100	    v0Tuples.add(tuple(f.seed, f.light, f.nutrient, f.founders));
   101	    v0Tuples.add(tuple(f.seed + v0.seedVariantOffset, f.light, f.nutrient, f.founders));
   102	  }
   103	  for (const r of v0Recipes) {
   104	    v0Seeds.add(r.seed);
   105	    v0Tuples.add(tuple(r.seed, r.recipe.family.light, r.recipe.family.nutrient, r.recipe.family.founders));
   106	  }
   107	  const v0Ids = new Set(v0.families.map((f) => f.id));
   108	  const ownSeeds = new Set<number>();
   109	  for (const f of confirm.families) {
   110	    if (v0Ids.has(f.id)) out.push(`family id ${f.id} is a v0 id`);
   111	    for (const s of [f.seed, f.seed + confirm.seedVariantOffset]) {
   112	      if (ownSeeds.has(s)) out.push(`seed ${s} is used twice within the confirm protocol`);
   113	      ownSeeds.add(s);
   114	      if (v0Seeds.has(s)) out.push(`seed ${s} (family ${f.id}) is a v0 seed`);
   115	      if (v0Tuples.has(tuple(s, f.light, f.nutrient, f.founders))) out.push(`identity of ${f.id} at seed ${s} equals a v0 simulation`);
   116	    }
   117	  }
   118	  if (confirm.salt === v0.salt) out.push("the salt equals v0's");
   119	  return out;
   120	}

   122	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   123	// The committed-amendment gate for the confirm run (a superset of confirmRefusal).
   124	
   125	export interface AmendmentGateInput {
   126	  protocol: Protocol;
   127	  freezeWorking: string | null;
   128	  protocolWorking: string;
   129	  amendmentWorking: string | null;
   130	  /** HEAD blob texts, null when HEAD has no such file. */
   131	  committed: { amendment: string | null; protocol: string | null; freeze: string | null };
   132	  /** `git status --porcelain` lines over the executable closure; any entry refuses. */
   133	  dirtyPaths: readonly string[];
   134	  outDirNonEmpty: boolean;
   135	  exploratory: boolean;
   136	}
   137	export function amendmentRefusal(i: AmendmentGateInput): string | null {
   138	  if (i.exploratory) return "--exploratory is refused with an amendment protocol: a rehearsal on the held-out families would spend them";
   139	  const f = freezeRefusal(i.protocol, i.freezeWorking);
   140	  if (f) return f.replace("protocol.json", "the confirm protocol").replace("protocol.freeze.sha256", "the confirm freeze file");
   141	  if (i.amendmentWorking === null) return "the amendment file is missing";
   142	  if (i.committed.amendment === null) return "the amendment is not committed (HEAD has no copy): commit the amendment and freeze before confirming";
   143	  if (i.committed.protocol === null) return "the confirm protocol is not committed (HEAD has no copy): commit the amendment and freeze before confirming";
   144	  if (i.committed.freeze === null) return "the confirm freeze file is not committed (HEAD has no copy): commit the amendment and freeze before confirming";
   145	  if (i.committed.amendment !== i.amendmentWorking) return "the amendment differs from its committed copy";
   146	  if (i.committed.protocol !== i.protocolWorking) return "the confirm protocol differs from its committed copy";
   147	  if (i.committed.freeze !== i.freezeWorking) return "the confirm freeze file differs from its committed copy";
   148	  if (i.dirtyPaths.length) return `the executable closure has uncommitted changes (${i.dirtyPaths.slice(0, 5).join(", ")}${i.dirtyPaths.length > 5 ? ", ..." : ""}): commit everything first`;
   149	  if (i.outDirNonEmpty) return "the output directory exists and is not empty: the first completed run is the attempt of record, never overwritten";
   150	  return null;
   151	}

   153	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   154	// The ledger: the committed, sufficient scoring input (test split, every non-oracle proposer).
   155	
   156	export type LedgerStatus = "answered" | "abstained" | "invalid" | "missing";
   157	export interface LedgerRow {
   158	  familyId: string;
   159	  caseId: string;
   160	  proposer: string;
   161	  status: LedgerStatus;
   162	  predicted: Direction3 | null;
   163	  probs: Probs | null;
   164	  observed: Direction3;
   165	  expected: Direction3;
   166	  measurementLimited: boolean;
   167	}
   168	
   169	export function buildLedger(p: Protocol, envelopes: readonly PredictionEnvelope[], lines: readonly PredictionLine[], rows: readonly CaseRow[]): LedgerRow[] {
   170	  const minEffect = p.minEffect!;
   171	  const test = rows.filter((r) => r.split === "test");
   172	  const ids = new Set(test.map((r) => r.caseId));
   173	  const envs = envelopes.filter((e) => ids.has(e.caseId));
   174	  const byRow = new Map(test.map((r) => [r.caseId, r]));
   175	  const out: LedgerRow[] = [];
   176	  for (const pr of buildProposers(p, envelopes).filter((x) => x.role !== "oracle")) {
   177	    const scored = scoredCases(lines, pr.id, envs, test, minEffect).sort((a, b) => (a.caseId < b.caseId ? -1 : 1));
   178	    for (const c of scored) {
   179	      const r = byRow.get(c.caseId)!;
   180	      out.push({
   181	        familyId: c.familyId,
   182	        caseId: c.caseId,
   183	        proposer: pr.id,
   184	        status: c.status,
   185	        predicted: c.status === "answered" ? argmax(c.prediction!.probs!) : null,
   186	        probs: c.status === "answered" ? c.prediction!.probs! : null,
   187	        observed: labelOf(r.effect.bodyMass, minEffect),
   188	        expected: r.expected,
   189	        measurementLimited: measurementLimited(r.effect.bodyMass, minEffect),
   190	      });
   191	    }
   192	  }
   193	  return out;
   194	}
   195	
   196	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   197	// The reading (AMENDMENT.md section 4), applied mechanically to the ledger alone.
   198	
   199	export const PRIMARY_RULE = "numericTrend";
   200	export const COMPARATORS = { vsShuffledEvidence: "shuffledEvidence", vsPriorMajority: "priorMajority" } as const;
   201	export const MAX_MEASUREMENT_LIMITED_SHARE = 0.25;
   202	
   203	export interface ReadingParams {
   204	  devPrior: Probs;
   205	  resamples: number;
   206	  seed: number;
   207	  /** meta.json status: only CONFIRM can be decisive. */
   208	  status: string;
   209	}
   210	export interface PairedResult {
   211	  comparator: string;
   212	  /** numericTrend minus comparator, imputed multiclass Brier, per-family means; negative favours numericTrend. */
   213	  interval: Interval | null;
   214	  nFamilies: number;
   215	  pairingComplete: boolean;
   216	}
   217	export type Label = "POSITIVE" | "NEGATIVE" | "UNINFORMATIVE";
   218	export interface Reading {
   219	  label: Label;
   220	  /** Descriptive suffix of an UNINFORMATIVE result from rule 4d; it never changes the label. */
   221	  suffix: string | null;
   222	  /** Rule 4a overrides that fired (empty when none). */
   223	  overrides: string[];
   224	  testFamilies: number;
   225	  testCases: number;
   226	  measurementLimitedShare: number | null;
   227	  comparisons: { vsShuffledEvidence: PairedResult; vsPriorMajority: PairedResult };
   228	  basis: string;
   229	}
   230	
   231	const imputedBrier = (r: LedgerRow, prior: Probs): number => (r.status === "answered" ? brierOf(r.probs!, r.observed) : brierOf(prior, r.observed));
   232	const rowsOf = (ledger: readonly LedgerRow[], proposer: string): LedgerRow[] => ledger.filter((r) => r.proposer === proposer);
   233	const sameSet = (a: Iterable<string>, b: Iterable<string>): boolean => {
   234	  const x = [...new Set(a)].sort(), y = [...new Set(b)].sort();
   235	  return x.length === y.length && x.every((v, i) => v === y[i]);
   236	};
   237	
   238	function paired(ledger: readonly LedgerRow[], comparator: string, prm: ReadingParams): PairedResult {
   239	  const nt = rowsOf(ledger, PRIMARY_RULE), cmp = rowsOf(ledger, comparator);
   240	  const pairingComplete = nt.length > 0 && nt.length === cmp.length && sameSet(nt.map((r) => r.familyId), cmp.map((r) => r.familyId)) && sameSet(nt.map((r) => `${r.familyId}|${r.caseId}`), cmp.map((r) => `${r.familyId}|${r.caseId}`));
   241	  const mean = (rs: LedgerRow[]) => familyMeans(rs.map((r) => ({ familyId: r.familyId, v: imputedBrier(r, prm.devPrior) })));
   242	  const diff = pairedFamilyDiff(mean(nt), mean(cmp));
   243	  return { comparator, interval: familyBootstrap(diff, prm.resamples, prm.seed), nFamilies: diff.size, pairingComplete };
   244	}
   245	
   246	const finite = (v: Interval | null): v is Interval => v !== null && Number.isFinite(v.lo) && Number.isFinite(v.hi) && Number.isFinite(v.mean);
   247	
   248	export function confirmReading(ledger: readonly LedgerRow[], prm: ReadingParams): Reading {
   249	  const nt = rowsOf(ledger, PRIMARY_RULE);
   250	  const families = new Set(nt.map((r) => r.familyId));
   251	  const A = paired(ledger, COMPARATORS.vsShuffledEvidence, prm), B = paired(ledger, COMPARATORS.vsPriorMajority, prm);
   252	  const cases = new Set(nt.map((r) => r.caseId));
   253	  const limited = new Set(nt.filter((r) => r.measurementLimited).map((r) => r.caseId));
   254	  const share = cases.size ? limited.size / cases.size : null;
   255	  const overrides: string[] = [];
   256	  if (families.size < MIN_FAMILIES_FOR_INTERVALS) overrides.push(`fewer than ${MIN_FAMILIES_FOR_INTERVALS} test families (${families.size})`);
   257	  if (prm.status !== "CONFIRM") overrides.push(`meta status is ${prm.status}, not CONFIRM`);
   258	  for (const r of [A, B]) {
   259	    if (!finite(r.interval)) overrides.push(`the interval vs ${r.comparator} is null or not finite`);
   260	    if (!r.pairingComplete) overrides.push(`the paired family or case sets of ${PRIMARY_RULE} and ${r.comparator} are not identical`);
   261	  }
   262	  const bad = nt.filter((r) => r.status === "invalid" || r.status === "missing").length;
   263	  if (bad > 0) overrides.push(`${PRIMARY_RULE} has ${bad} invalid or missing predictions`);
   264	  const answered = nt.filter((r) => r.status === "answered");
   265	  const classes = new Set(answered.map((r) => r.predicted));
   266	  if (answered.length === 0) overrides.push(`${PRIMARY_RULE} answered no test case`);
   267	  else if (classes.size === 1) overrides.push(`${PRIMARY_RULE} is degenerate: one predicted class (${[...classes][0]}) on all ${answered.length} answered test cases`);
   268	  for (const c of Object.values(COMPARATORS)) {
   269	    const badC = rowsOf(ledger, c).filter((r) => r.status === "invalid" || r.status === "missing").length;
   270	    if (badC > 0) overrides.push(`${c} has ${badC} invalid or missing predictions`);
   271	  }
   272	  if (share !== null && share > MAX_MEASUREMENT_LIMITED_SHARE) overrides.push(`measurement-limited cases are ${(share * 100).toFixed(1)}% of test cases (limit ${MAX_MEASUREMENT_LIMITED_SHARE * 100}%)`);
   273	  const base = { suffix: null as string | null, testFamilies: families.size, testCases: cases.size, measurementLimitedShare: share, comparisons: { vsShuffledEvidence: A, vsPriorMajority: B } };
   274	  if (overrides.length) return { ...base, label: "UNINFORMATIVE", overrides, basis: "rule 4a: an override fired" };
   275	  const a = A.interval!, b = B.interval!;
   276	  if (a.lo > 0 || b.lo > 0) return { ...base, label: "NEGATIVE", overrides, basis: "rule 4b: a primary interval lies wholly above 0" };
   277	  if (a.hi < 0 && b.hi < 0) return { ...base, label: "POSITIVE", overrides, basis: "rule 4c: both primary intervals lie wholly below 0" };
   278	  return { ...base, label: "UNINFORMATIVE", overrides, suffix: b.mean >= 0 ? "no improvement shown" : "improvement not supported", basis: "rule 4d: neither NEGATIVE nor POSITIVE" };
   279	}
   280	
   281	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   282	// The sealed decision document.
   283	
   284	export interface DescriptiveRow {
   285	  proposer: string;
   286	  n: number;
   287	  answered: number;
   288	  abstained: number;
   289	  invalid: number;
   290	  missing: number;
   291	  coverage: number;
   292	  brierImputed: number | null;
   293	  directionalAccuracy: number | null;
   294	  predictedClasses: Record<Direction3, number>;
   295	  /** Share of answered cases whose predicted class equals the fixture's expected label. */
   296	  labelAgreementWithExpectation: number | null;
   297	}
   298	export interface FailedPrediction {
   299	  caseId: string;
   300	  familyId: string;
   301	  status: LedgerStatus;
   302	  predicted: Direction3 | null;
   303	  observed: Direction3;
   304	}
   305	export interface ConfirmationDoc {
   306	  kind: "prediction-confirmation";
   307	  version: 1;
   308	  status: string;
   309	  protocolSha256: string;
   310	  primaryRule: string;
   311	  params: { resamples: number; seed: number; devPrior: Probs; minFamilies: number; maxMeasurementLimitedShare: number };
   312	  reading: Reading;
   313	  /** Descriptive only: cannot produce a POSITIVE or NEGATIVE claim. */
   314	  descriptive: DescriptiveRow[];
   315	  /** numericTrend's failed predictions on the test split (wrong argmax, invalid or missing); abstentions are not failures. */
   316	  failedPredictions: FailedPrediction[];
   317	}
   318	
   319	export function describeLedger(ledger: readonly LedgerRow[], prior: Probs): DescriptiveRow[] {
   320	  const ids = [...new Set(ledger.map((r) => r.proposer))];
   321	  return ids.map((id) => {
   322	    const rs = rowsOf(ledger, id);
   323	    const ans = rs.filter((r) => r.status === "answered");
   324	    const predictedClasses: Record<Direction3, number> = { up: 0, down: 0, none: 0 };
   325	    for (const r of ans) predictedClasses[r.predicted!]++;
   326	    return {
   327	      proposer: id,
   328	      n: rs.length,
   329	      answered: ans.length,
   330	      abstained: rs.filter((r) => r.status === "abstained").length,
   331	      invalid: rs.filter((r) => r.status === "invalid").length,
   332	      missing: rs.filter((r) => r.status === "missing").length,
   333	      coverage: rs.length ? ans.length / rs.length : 0,
   334	      brierImputed: rs.length ? rs.reduce((s, r) => s + imputedBrier(r, prior), 0) / rs.length : null,
   335	      directionalAccuracy: ans.length ? ans.filter((r) => r.predicted === r.observed).length / ans.length : null,
   336	      predictedClasses,
   337	      labelAgreementWithExpectation: ans.length ? ans.filter((r) => r.predicted === r.expected).length / ans.length : null,
   338	    };
   339	  });
   340	}
   341	
   342	export function buildConfirmation(p: Protocol, status: string, ledger: readonly LedgerRow[]): ConfirmationDoc {
   343	  const prm: ReadingParams = { devPrior: p.devPrior!, resamples: p.bootstrap.resamples, seed: p.bootstrap.seed, status };
   344	  const failedPredictions = rowsOf(ledger, PRIMARY_RULE)
   345	    .filter((r) => r.status === "invalid" || r.status === "missing" || (r.status === "answered" && r.predicted !== r.observed))
   346	    .map((r) => ({ caseId: r.caseId, familyId: r.familyId, status: r.status, predicted: r.predicted, observed: r.observed }));
   347	  return {
   348	    kind: "prediction-confirmation",
   349	    version: 1,
   350	    status,
   351	    protocolSha256: sha256Text(canonical(p)),
   352	    primaryRule: PRIMARY_RULE,
   353	    params: { resamples: prm.resamples, seed: prm.seed, devPrior: prm.devPrior, minFamilies: MIN_FAMILIES_FOR_INTERVALS, maxMeasurementLimitedShare: MAX_MEASUREMENT_LIMITED_SHARE },
   354	    reading: confirmReading(ledger, prm),
   355	    descriptive: describeLedger(ledger, prm.devPrior),
   356	    failedPredictions,
   357	  };
   358	}
   359	
   360	/** The sha256 file over the sealed decision and the ledger. */
   361	export const confirmDigest = (confirmationText: string, ledgerText: string): string => `${sha256Text(confirmationText)}  confirmation.json\n${sha256Text(ledgerText)}  ledger.jsonl\n`;
     1	// I02b: the fresh-family recipe, the confirm protocol, the committed-amendment gate and the mechanical reading
     2	// (docs/prediction-confirm-2026-11-02/PLAN.md, section 7). No confirm family is ever simulated here.
     3	import { spawnSync } from "node:child_process";
     4	import { existsSync, mkdtempSync, readFileSync, rmSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
     5	import { tmpdir } from "node:os";
     6	import { join } from "node:path";
     7	import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
     8	import { afterAll, describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
     9	import { canonical } from "../lib/propose.ts";
    10	import { COMPARATORS, buildConfirmProtocol, buildConfirmation, confirmDigest, confirmReading, disjointnessProblems, freshFamilies, identityProblems, amendmentRefusal, recipeTupleProblems, type AmendmentGateInput, type LedgerRow, type ReadingParams, type V0RecipeRow } from "../lib/prediction/confirm.ts";
    11	import { protocolHash, splitPlanOf, type Protocol } from "../lib/prediction/run.ts";
    12	import { assertNoLeak } from "../lib/prediction/splits.ts";
    13	import { renderReport } from "../lib/prediction/report-html.ts";
    14	import { sha256Text } from "../lib/propose.ts";

    17	const root = fileURLToPath(new URL("../..", import.meta.url));
    18	const EXP = join(root, "experiments/prediction-envelope");
    19	const readJson = <T>(p: string): T => JSON.parse(readFileSync(p, "utf8")) as T;
    20	const v0 = readJson<Protocol>(join(EXP, "protocol.json"));
    21	const confirm = readJson<Protocol>(join(EXP, "protocol.confirm.json"));
    22	const v0Recipes = readFileSync(join(EXP, "v0/recipes.jsonl"), "utf8").split("\n").filter((l) => l.trim() !== "").map((l) => JSON.parse(l) as V0RecipeRow);
    23	
    24	describe("fresh families", () => {
    25	  it("fresh families are disjoint from every v0 family", () => {
    26	    expect(disjointnessProblems(confirm, v0, v0Recipes)).toEqual([]);
    27	    // explicit, independent of the helper
    28	    const used = new Set<number>();
    29	    for (const f of v0.families) used.add(f.seed).add(f.seed + v0.seedVariantOffset);
    30	    for (const r of v0Recipes) used.add(r.seed);
    31	    for (const f of confirm.families) {
    32	      expect(used.has(f.seed)).toBe(false);
    33	      expect(used.has(f.seed + confirm.seedVariantOffset)).toBe(false);
    34	      expect(v0.families.some((g) => g.id === f.id)).toBe(false);
    35	    }
    36	    expect(confirm.salt).not.toBe(v0.salt);
    37	  });
    38	  it("the helper catches reuse of a v0 seed, a v0 variant seed, a v0 id and the v0 salt", () => {
    39	    const bad = structuredClone(confirm);
    40	    bad.families[0].seed = 5003;
    41	    bad.families[1].seed = 5003 + 7919 - 7919 + 7919;
    42	    bad.families[2].id = "f05";
    43	    bad.salt = v0.salt;
    44	    const problems = disjointnessProblems(bad, v0, v0Recipes).join("\n");
    45	    expect(problems).toMatch(/seed 5003/);
    46	    expect(problems).toMatch(/id f05/);
    47	    expect(problems).toMatch(/salt/);
    48	  });
    49	  it("confirm protocol differs from the frozen protocol only in identity fields", () => {
    50	    expect(identityProblems(confirm, v0)).toEqual([]);
    51	    expect(recipeTupleProblems(confirm, v0)).toEqual([]);
    52	    expect(canonical(confirm)).not.toBe(canonical(v0));
    53	    // the committed file is exactly what the recipe builds, and its freeze file is its hash
    54	    expect(canonical(confirm)).toBe(canonical(buildConfirmProtocol(v0)));
    55	    expect(readFileSync(join(EXP, "protocol.confirm.freeze.sha256"), "utf8")).toBe(protocolHash(confirm) + "\n");
    56	    expect(confirm.frozen).toBe(true);
    57	    expect(confirm.minEffect).toBe(25);
    58	    expect(confirm.devPrior).toEqual(v0.devPrior);
    59	    expect(freshFamilies()).toEqual(confirm.families);
    60	    // v0's frozen files stay as frozen
    61	    expect(readFileSync(join(EXP, "protocol.freeze.sha256"), "utf8").trim()).toBe(protocolHash(v0));
    62	  });
    63	  it("an edit outside the identity fields, or a changed recipe tuple, is caught", () => {
    64	    const m = structuredClone(confirm);
    65	    m.minEffect = 26;
    66	    m.window.horizon = 201;
    67	    expect(identityProblems(m, v0).sort()).toEqual(["minEffect", "window"]);
    68	    const t = structuredClone(confirm);
    69	    t.families[3].founders += 1;
    70	    t.families[16].nutrient = 7;
    71	    expect(recipeTupleProblems(t, v0)).toHaveLength(2);
    72	  });
    73	  it("split plan: 16 test families (12 original, 4 rising), no family in two splits, donors stay within split, assertNoLeak clean", () => {
    74	    const plan = splitPlanOf(confirm);
    75	    expect(plan.test).toHaveLength(16);
    76	    expect(plan.dev).toHaveLength(4);
    77	    expect(plan.qualify).toEqual([]);
    78	    const rising = new Set(confirm.families.filter((f) => f.cohort === "rising").map((f) => f.id));
    79	    expect(plan.test.filter((f) => rising.has(f))).toHaveLength(4);
    80	    expect(plan.test.filter((f) => !rising.has(f))).toHaveLength(12);
    81	    expect(plan.test.length * 6).toBe(96);
    82	    expect(plan.dev.filter((f) => plan.test.includes(f))).toEqual([]);
    83	    expect(new Set([...plan.dev, ...plan.test]).size).toBe(20);
    84	    const devSet = new Set(plan.dev);
    85	    for (const [f, d] of Object.entries(confirm.donors)) {
    86	      expect(f).not.toBe(d);
    87	      expect(devSet.has(f)).toBe(devSet.has(d));
    88	    }
    89	    expect(assertNoLeak(plan, confirm.donors, [])).toEqual([]);
    90	    expect(plan.test.length).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(10);
    91	  });
    92	});
    93	
    94	describe("the committed-amendment gate", () => {
    95	  const h = protocolHash(confirm) + "\n";
    96	  const pj = JSON.stringify(confirm);
    97	  const ok = (): AmendmentGateInput => ({ protocol: confirm, freezeWorking: h, protocolWorking: pj, amendmentWorking: "A", committed: { amendment: "A", protocol: pj, freeze: h }, dirtyPaths: [], outDirNonEmpty: false, exploratory: false });
    98	  it("passes when everything matches", () => {
    99	    expect(amendmentRefusal(ok())).toBeNull();
   100	  });
   101	  it("gate refuses", () => {
   102	    const r = (m: (i: AmendmentGateInput) => void): string | null => {
   103	      const i = ok();
   104	      m(i);
   105	      return amendmentRefusal(i);
   106	    };
   107	    expect(r((i) => (i.exploratory = true))).toMatch(/exploratory/);
   108	    expect(r((i) => (i.freezeWorking = null))).toMatch(/missing/);
   109	    expect(r((i) => (i.freezeWorking = "0".repeat(64)))).toMatch(/does not match/);
   110	    expect(r((i) => (i.protocol = { ...confirm, minEffect: 26 }))).toMatch(/does not match/);
   111	    expect(r((i) => (i.protocol = { ...confirm, frozen: false }))).toMatch(/not frozen/);
   112	    expect(r((i) => (i.amendmentWorking = null))).toMatch(/amendment file is missing/);
   113	    expect(r((i) => (i.committed.amendment = null))).toMatch(/amendment is not committed/);
   114	    expect(r((i) => (i.committed.protocol = null))).toMatch(/protocol is not committed/);
   115	    expect(r((i) => (i.committed.freeze = null))).toMatch(/freeze file is not committed/);
   116	    expect(r((i) => (i.committed.amendment = "B"))).toMatch(/amendment differs/);
   117	    expect(r((i) => (i.committed.protocol = pj + " "))).toMatch(/protocol differs/);
   118	    expect(r((i) => (i.committed.freeze = "x"))).toMatch(/freeze file differs/);
   119	    expect(r((i) => (i.dirtyPaths = [" M tools/predict.ts"]))).toMatch(/uncommitted changes \( ?M tools\/predict.ts/);
   120	    expect(r((i) => (i.outDirNonEmpty = true))).toMatch(/not empty/);
   121	  });
   122	});
   123	
   124	describe("the CLI honours --protocol", () => {
   125	  const dir = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), "predict-confirm-"));
   126	  afterAll(() => rmSync(dir, { recursive: true, force: true }));
   127	  const deno = (args: string[]) => spawnSync("deno", ["run", "--no-lock", "-A", "tools/predict.ts", ...args], { cwd: root, encoding: "utf8" });
   128	  it("refuses the confirm protocol with --exploratory, with no amendment, and with an uncommitted amendment; never writes output", () => {
   129	    const out = join(dir, "o1");
   130	    const base = ["run", "--stage", "confirm", "--protocol", join(EXP, "protocol.confirm.json"), "--freeze", join(EXP, "protocol.confirm.freeze.sha256"), "--out", out];
   131	    const a = deno([...base, "--amendment", join(dir, "missing.md")]);
   132	    expect(a.status).toBe(1);
   133	    expect(a.stderr).toMatch(/refusing to confirm/);
   134	    const b = deno(["run", "--stage", "confirm", "--protocol", join(EXP, "protocol.confirm.json"), "--out", out]);
   135	    expect(b.status).toBe(1);
   136	    expect(b.stderr).toMatch(/needs --freeze and --amendment/);
   137	    const c = deno([...base, "--amendment", join(dir, "missing.md"), "--exploratory", "yes"]);
   138	    expect(c.status).toBe(1);
   139	    expect(c.stderr).toMatch(/exploratory/);
   140	    const d = deno(["run", "--stage", "dev", "--protocol", join(EXP, "protocol.confirm.json"), "--out", out]);
   141	    expect(d.status).toBe(1);
   142	    expect(existsSync(out)).toBe(false);
   143	  }, 120_000);
   144	  it("protocol-confirm never overwrites", () => {
   145	    const r = deno(["protocol-confirm"]);
   146	    expect(r.status).toBe(1);
   147	    expect(r.stderr).toMatch(/not overwriting/);
   148	  }, 120_000);
   149	});
   150	
   151	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   152	// The reading, on synthetic ledgers.
   153	
   154	const prior = { up: 135, down: 271, none: 594 };
   155	const prm: ReadingParams = { devPrior: prior, resamples: 400, seed: 7, status: "CONFIRM" };
   156	type Cls = "up" | "down" | "none";
   157	const P = (c: Cls, conf: number): { up: number; down: number; none: number } => {
   158	  const rest = Math.floor((1000 - conf) / 2);
   159	  const o = { up: rest, down: rest, none: rest };
   160	  o[c] = 1000 - 2 * rest;
   161	  return o;
   162	};
   163	interface Spec {
   164	  families?: number;
   165	  casesPerFamily?: number;
   166	  observed: (i: number, f: number) => Cls;
   167	  nt: (i: number, f: number, o: Cls) => LedgerRow["probs"];
   168	  shuffled?: (i: number, f: number, o: Cls) => LedgerRow["probs"];
   169	  prior?: (i: number, f: number, o: Cls) => LedgerRow["probs"];
   170	  limited?: (i: number, f: number) => boolean;
   171	}
   172	function ledgerOf(s: Spec): LedgerRow[] {
   173	  const F = s.families ?? 12, C = s.casesPerFamily ?? 6;
   174	  const rows: LedgerRow[] = [];
   175	  for (let f = 0; f < F; f++)
   176	    for (let i = 0; i < C; i++) {
   177	      const o = s.observed(i, f);
   178	      const mk = (proposer: string, probs: LedgerRow["probs"]): LedgerRow => ({
   179	        familyId: `c${f}`,
   180	        caseId: `k${f}-${i}`,
   181	        proposer,
   182	        status: probs ? "answered" : "missing",
   183	        predicted: probs ? (Object.entries(probs).sort((a, b) => b[1] - a[1])[0][0] as Cls) : null,
   184	        probs,
   185	        observed: o,
   186	        expected: o,
   187	        measurementLimited: s.limited?.(i, f) ?? false,
   188	      });
   189	      rows.push(mk("numericTrend", s.nt(i, f, o)), mk("shuffledEvidence", (s.shuffled ?? (() => prior))(i, f, o)), mk("priorMajority", (s.prior ?? (() => prior))(i, f, o)));
   190	    }
   191	  return rows;
   192	}
   193	const cyc = (i: number): Cls => (["up", "down", "none"] as const)[i % 3];
   194	
   195	describe("reading", () => {
   196	  // numericTrend right with confidence on every case; the controls are poor.
   197	  const good = (): Spec => ({ observed: (i) => cyc(i), nt: (i, _f, o) => P(o, 800), shuffled: (i) => P(cyc(i + 1), 800), prior: () => prior });
   198	  it("POSITIVE when both intervals lie wholly below 0", () => {
   199	    const r = confirmReading(ledgerOf(good()), prm);
   200	    expect(r.overrides).toEqual([]);
   201	    expect(r.label).toBe("POSITIVE");
   202	    expect(r.comparisons.vsShuffledEvidence.interval!.hi).toBeLessThan(0);
   203	    expect(r.comparisons.vsPriorMajority.interval!.hi).toBeLessThan(0);
   204	  });
   205	  it("NEGATIVE when numericTrend is overconfidently wrong: interval wholly above 0", () => {
   206	    const l = ledgerOf({ observed: (i) => cyc(i), nt: (i) => P(cyc(i + 1), 900), shuffled: (i) => P(cyc(i + 1), 900), prior: () => prior });
   207	    const r = confirmReading(l, prm);
   208	    expect(r.label).toBe("NEGATIVE");
   209	    expect(r.comparisons.vsPriorMajority.interval!.lo).toBeGreaterThan(0);
   210	  });
   211	  it("negative fixture fools a naive metric: high directional accuracy, still NEGATIVE by Brier; and an always-none predictor is degenerate, not POSITIVE", () => {
   212	    const sure = (c: Cls): LedgerRow["probs"] => ({ up: c === "up" ? 998 : 1, down: c === "down" ? 998 : 1, none: c === "none" ? 998 : 1 });
   213	    // 12 cases per family: 10 none, 1 up, 1 down. numericTrend says none (998) on the none cases and is confidently wrong on the other two.
   214	    const obs = (i: number): Cls => (i === 10 ? "up" : i === 11 ? "down" : "none");
   215	    const l = ledgerOf({ casesPerFamily: 12, observed: obs, nt: (i) => (i === 10 ? sure("down") : i === 11 ? sure("up") : sure("none")), shuffled: () => prior, prior: () => ({ up: 100, down: 100, none: 800 }) });
   216	    const nt = l.filter((r) => r.proposer === "numericTrend");
   217	    expect(new Set(nt.map((r) => r.predicted)).size).toBe(3);
   218	    expect(nt.filter((r) => r.predicted === r.observed).length / nt.length).toBeGreaterThan(0.8);
   219	    const r = confirmReading(l, prm);
   220	    expect(r.overrides).toEqual([]);
   221	    expect(r.comparisons.vsPriorMajority.interval!.lo).toBeGreaterThan(0);
   222	    expect(r.label).toBe("NEGATIVE");
   223	    // always none: high accuracy on a none-heavy set, but degenerate => UNINFORMATIVE by override, never POSITIVE
   224	    const z = confirmReading(ledgerOf({ observed: () => "none", nt: () => sure("none"), shuffled: () => P("down", 800), prior: () => P("down", 800) }), prm);
   225	    expect(z.label).toBe("UNINFORMATIVE");
   226	    expect(z.overrides.join(" ")).toMatch(/degenerate/);
   227	  });
   228	  // numericTrend equals the prior except on i%3==0 cases, where it says up with {400,300,300}: better when the case is up (good family), worse when it is down.
   229	  const mixed = (good: (f: number) => boolean, bad: LedgerRow["probs"]): Spec => ({ observed: (i, f) => (i % 3 === 0 ? (good(f) ? "up" : "down") : "none"), nt: (i, f) => (i % 3 !== 0 ? prior : good(f) ? { up: 400, down: 300, none: 300 } : bad), shuffled: () => prior, prior: () => prior });
   230	  it("UNINFORMATIVE ('no improvement shown') when the point estimate is positive but the interval spans 0; a non-negative point estimate alone is never NEGATIVE", () => {
   231	    const r = confirmReading(ledgerOf(mixed((f) => f % 2 === 0, { up: 800, down: 20, none: 180 })), prm);
   232	    expect(r.overrides).toEqual([]);
   233	    expect(r.comparisons.vsPriorMajority.interval!.mean).toBeGreaterThan(0);
   234	    expect(r.comparisons.vsPriorMajority.interval!.lo).toBeLessThan(0);
   235	    expect(r.label).toBe("UNINFORMATIVE");
   236	    expect(r.suffix).toBe("no improvement shown");
   237	  });
   238	  it("UNINFORMATIVE ('improvement not supported') when the point estimate is negative but the interval reaches 0", () => {
   239	    const r = confirmReading(ledgerOf(mixed((f) => f < 2, { up: 150, down: 230, none: 620 })), prm);
   240	    expect(r.overrides).toEqual([]);
   241	    expect(r.comparisons.vsPriorMajority.interval!.mean).toBeLessThan(0);
   242	    expect(r.comparisons.vsPriorMajority.interval!.hi).toBeGreaterThan(0);
   243	    expect(r.label).toBe("UNINFORMATIVE");
   244	    expect(r.suffix).toBe("improvement not supported");
   245	  });
   246	  it("each override fires, and an override beats NEGATIVE", () => {
   247	    const bad = (): Spec => ({ observed: (i) => cyc(i), nt: (i) => P(cyc(i + 1), 900), shuffled: (i) => P(cyc(i + 1), 900), prior: () => prior });
   248	    expect(confirmReading(ledgerOf(bad()), prm).label).toBe("NEGATIVE");
   249	    expect(confirmReading(ledgerOf({ ...bad(), families: 9 }), prm)).toMatchObject({ label: "UNINFORMATIVE", overrides: expect.arrayContaining([expect.stringMatching(/fewer than 10/)]) });
   250	    expect(confirmReading(ledgerOf(bad()), { ...prm, status: "EXPLORATORY" })).toMatchObject({ label: "UNINFORMATIVE", overrides: expect.arrayContaining([expect.stringMatching(/not CONFIRM/)]) });
   251	    // incomplete pairing: drop one shuffledEvidence row
   252	    const l = ledgerOf(bad());
   253	    const i = l.findIndex((r) => r.proposer === "shuffledEvidence");
   254	    const cut = [...l.slice(0, i), ...l.slice(i + 1)];
   255	    expect(confirmReading(cut, prm)).toMatchObject({ label: "UNINFORMATIVE", overrides: expect.arrayContaining([expect.stringMatching(/not identical/)]) });
   256	    // invalid numericTrend prediction
   257	    const inv = ledgerOf(bad()).map((r, k) => (r.proposer === "numericTrend" && k === 0 ? { ...r, status: "invalid" as const, probs: null, predicted: null } : r));
   258	    expect(confirmReading(inv, prm)).toMatchObject({ label: "UNINFORMATIVE", overrides: expect.arrayContaining([expect.stringMatching(/invalid or missing/)]) });
   259	    // invalid comparator prediction
   260	    const invC = ledgerOf(bad()).map((r) => (r.proposer === "priorMajority" && r.caseId === "k0-0" ? { ...r, status: "invalid" as const, probs: null, predicted: null } : r));
   261	    expect(confirmReading(invC, prm).overrides.join(" ")).toMatch(/priorMajority has 1 invalid/);
   262	    // zero answered
   263	    const zero = ledgerOf(bad()).map((r) => (r.proposer === "numericTrend" ? { ...r, status: "abstained" as const, probs: null, predicted: null } : r));
   264	    expect(confirmReading(zero, prm).overrides.join(" ")).toMatch(/answered no test case/);
   265	    // degenerate
   266	    expect(confirmReading(ledgerOf({ ...bad(), nt: () => P("none", 900) }), prm).overrides.join(" ")).toMatch(/degenerate/);
   267	    // more than 25% measurement-limited
   268	    const lim = confirmReading(ledgerOf({ ...bad(), limited: (i) => i < 2 }), prm);
   269	    expect(lim.label).toBe("UNINFORMATIVE");
   270	    expect(lim.overrides.join(" ")).toMatch(/measurement-limited/);
   271	    // exactly 25% is not over the limit
   272	    expect(confirmReading(ledgerOf({ ...bad(), casesPerFamily: 8, limited: (i) => i < 2 }), prm).overrides.join(" ")).not.toMatch(/measurement-limited/);
   273	    // null interval: all families identical => bootstrap defined; a null interval comes from < 10 families
   274	    expect(confirmReading([], prm).label).toBe("UNINFORMATIVE");
   275	  });
   276	  it("the sealed document recomputes from its ledger alone and the digest names both files", () => {
   277	    const l = ledgerOf(good());
   278	    const doc = buildConfirmation({ ...confirm, devPrior: prior, bootstrap: { resamples: 400, seed: 7 } } as Protocol, "CONFIRM", l);
   279	    const again = confirmReading(JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(l)) as LedgerRow[], prm);
   280	    expect(JSON.parse(canonical(doc.reading))).toEqual(JSON.parse(canonical(again)));
   281	    expect(doc.failedPredictions).toEqual([]);
   282	    expect(COMPARATORS.vsPriorMajority).toBe("priorMajority");
   283	    expect(confirmDigest("a", "b")).toBe(`${sha256Text("a")}  confirmation.json\n${sha256Text("b")}  ledger.jsonl\n`);
   284	  });
   285	});
   286	
   287	describe("the notebook", () => {
   288	  it("report lists failed predictions first and shows CONFIRM status and the reading table", () => {
   289	    const { p, built } = tinyPipeline();
   290	    const read = (n: string): string => built.files[n];
   291	    const lines = read("predictions.jsonl").split("\n").filter(Boolean).map((l) => JSON.parse(l));
   292	    const envelopes = read("envelopes.jsonl").split("\n").filter(Boolean).map((l) => JSON.parse(l));
   293	    const rows = read("outcomes.jsonl").split("\n").filter(Boolean).map((l) => JSON.parse(l));
   294	    const ledgerFake: LedgerRow[] = ledgerOf({ observed: (i) => cyc(i), nt: (i, f) => (f === 0 && i === 0 ? P("down", 900) : P(cyc(i), 900)), shuffled: (i) => P(cyc(i + 1), 800) });
   295	    const doc = buildConfirmation({ ...p, devPrior: prior, bootstrap: { resamples: 200, seed: 3 } } as Protocol, "CONFIRM", ledgerFake);
   296	    expect(doc.failedPredictions.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
   297	    const html = renderReport({ comparison: built.comparison, envelopes, lines, rows, minEffect: p.minEffect!, proposerIds: ["priorMajority", "numericTrend"], confirmation: doc });
   298	    expect(html).toMatch(/Status: CONFIRM/);
   299	    expect(html).toMatch(/Reading: (POSITIVE|NEGATIVE|UNINFORMATIVE)/);
   300	    expect(html.indexOf("Failed predictions of numericTrend")).toBeGreaterThan(0);
   301	    expect(html.indexOf("Failed predictions of numericTrend")).toBeLessThan(html.indexOf("<h2>Comparison</h2>"));
   302	    expect(html.indexOf("Primary comparisons")).toBeLessThan(html.indexOf("<h2>Cases</h2>"));
   303	    expect(html).not.toMatch(/<script|https?:\/\//i);
   304	    // held-out test cases come before dev cases in the case list, failed predictions first within each
   305	    const caseSplits = [...html.matchAll(/<small>family \S+, (dev|test)<\/small>/g)].map((m) => m[1]);
   306	    expect(caseSplits.indexOf("test")).toBe(0);
   307	    expect(caseSplits.lastIndexOf("test")).toBeLessThan(caseSplits.indexOf("dev"));
   308	    // without a confirmation the report is the v0 notebook

+function confirmationHtml(d: ConfirmationDoc): string {
