    85	### I02 — A naturalist that predicts interventions
    86	
    87	**Question.** Can a model identify a causal mechanism well enough to predict an intervention, beyond what numerical baselines predict? **Sources:** [ASAL](https://pub.sakana.ai/asal/), [FER](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11581).
    88	
    89	**Experience.** A field note says, “I expect this patch to lose its advantage if signal is disabled,” with a confidence and a proposed paired replay. The notebook subsequently records whether the prediction survived. Observations, hypotheses and measured outcomes are visibly distinct.
    90	
    91	**Implementation.** Extend the existing naturalist bench and suggestion pipeline. Its input is a fixed evidence envelope: registered frame samples, census series, configuration and an allowed intervention menu. Its output is structured data containing subject/time, predicted direction, readout, intervention, confidence and an abstention option. A host validator permits only predefined data operations. Use the discovery campaign format where compatible; the current CPU case runner is not automatically a general naturalist benchmark.
    92	
    93	**Experiment.** Blind the proposer to intervention outcomes. Compare numeric-only, video-only, combined inputs, a fixed rule proposer and shuffled evidence on identical cases. Split whole source histories between development and final testing. Do not let multiple windows from the same run leak across splits. Evaluate on already-known mechanism fixtures first, then unfamiliar cases.
    94	
    95	**Readouts.** Proper probability scoring (Brier score), directional accuracy, abstention/coverage, invalid proposals, outcome effect size, cost per useful experiment and counterfactual agreement. Equalise available evidence and intervention budget. An expensive model must improve decisions, not merely write more interesting descriptions.
    96	
    97	**Advance/stop.** Require better prospective prediction than the strongest simple baseline and stable calibration on held-out histories. If it only captions, retain a clearly labelled narration feature. Do not extend existing neutral-bell calibration to new worlds without recalibration. **Deliverable:** prediction/outcome JSONL, comparison table and a notebook view of failed as well as successful predictions.
    98	
    99	### I03 — Historical-opponent ecology
   100	
   101	**Question.** Does later evolution produce broader competitive ability, specialised counter-strategies, or mere replacement? **Sources:** [Digital Red Queen](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.03335), [Core War](https://corewar.co.uk/).
   102	
   103	**Implementation.** Archive genotypes or explicitly defined population samples at fixed times, then compete them against a frozen panel spanning ancestral and intermediate opponents. Reuse mutation replay and discovery tooling. Begin with genome-only, standard-inoculum common gardens; test ecological context separately. Moving a whole patch also moves stored resources, partners and developmental state, so that is a different experiment.
   104	
   105	**Experiment.** Use reciprocal positions and starting ratios, mutation-off assays, independent source histories, identical budgets and a genotype-neutral two-label control. Start from a small panel of known distinct strategies to establish assay sensitivity. Include isolated-growth performance so “wins” caused solely by general vigour are distinguishable from matchup-specific advantages. If evolving against an archive is later tested, compare recent-only, fixed-panel and historical-mixture opponents at matched compute.
   106	
   107	**Readouts.** Full pairwise payoff matrices with uncertainty, invasion from rare, extinction, generality on opponents withheld from training, and cycles such as A beats B, B beats C, C beats A. Report diversity independently from mean win rate. Account for synthesis versus lottery relabelling where the exact observer permits it; label unresolved attribution rather than claiming predation.
   108	
   109	**Advance/stop.** Do not run a long tournament if both candidates routinely hit the extinction floor. First fix the assay or report it uninformative. A positive result establishes competitive change in the tested environment, not organism reproduction or open-endedness. **Deliverable:** replayable matchup bundles and an interactive time-versus-opponent matrix. No predation rule is required for the first test.
   110	
   111	### I04 — Repair across unfamiliar wounds
   112	
   240	
   241	**Advance/stop.** Use this suite to reject weak metrics before they enter I01 or I07. It earns no open-endedness claim. **Deliverable:** a fast deterministic fixture library and an accessible “why this is not yet evidence of life” comparison page.
   242	
   243	## Agent execution contract
   244	
   245	### Scope and invariants
   246	
   247	These are idea plans, not authorisation to execute every study. The current task produces documents only. A future implementation should choose one ID, restate its bounded question, inspect the then-current checkout and reserve its own experiment identity. Prior budgets in historical notes are not new spending authority.
   248	
   249	Preserve default config hashes for observer-only changes. Intended dynamics changes require the repository's rule-version procedure and deliberate golden-hash changes; do not hide a dynamics change under an innocent-looking optional key. Optional settings must remain absent from `defaultConfig()` when preserving existing serialized defaults. A genuinely new substrate gets an explicitly separate rule/schema identity rather than borrowing the chemistry engine's claims.
   250	
   251	CPU and WGSL implementations must agree bit for bit for shared integer rules. Extend arithmetic bounds in `docs/rules.md`, validation and overflow fixtures before adding an operation. Keep checkpoint physics and observer state paired under their digest, and prove segmented equivalence for new checkpointed state. Do not weaken inferred-individuality claims by switching on declared-cell rules without labelling the experiment.
   252	
   253	Current optional capability genes and inherited kernel shape share a layout byte: `genes` and `shapeReach` cannot be combined without a layout migration. Gene configurations also reject the existing neutral and declared-cell modes; choose valid, explicitly described controls rather than bypassing validation.
   254	
   255	The preregistration is frozen. Add a dated exploratory protocol or amendment outside its generated frozen sections. A score used to choose candidates cannot also be presented as independent confirmation. New metrics need versioned definitions; new presets need their own baseline qualification. Analysis streams large records and never assumes a complete `lineages.tsv` fits in RAM.
   256	
   257	### Common protocol to fill before the first scientific run
   258	
   259	| Field | Required content |
   260	|---|---|
   261	| Identity | Idea ID; question; repo SHA; rule/schema/metric versions; full config and preset identities; hashes of founders, candidate genomes and observation code |
   262	| Sampling | Independent unit; development/screening/confirmation splits; seed reservations; inclusion/exclusion rules; no cross-split source histories |
   263	| Intervention | Exact operation, time, spatial rule, before/after inventories, resource destination and sham |
   264	| Estimand | One primary outcome and effect threshold; secondary outcomes; expected direction; why the assay is sensitive |
   265	| Design | Arms, pairing, position swaps where applicable, number of independent blocks, horizon and cadence |
   266	| Decision | Fixed-sample or prespecified sequential test; multiplicity control; extinction/censoring; confidence intervals; measurement-limited category |
   267	| Provenance | Commands, hardware/backend, source/model/encoder versions, prompts when used, selection history, failed attempts and immutable input manifest |
   268	| Budget | Benchmark steps/s on the actual device; proposal/evaluation counts; model tokens/cost if relevant; stop cap and checkpoint/restart policy |
   269	| Interpretation | What a positive, negative or uninformative outcome establishes, and what it cannot establish |
   270	
   271	No generic sample count makes every study adequate. Calibrate assay sensitivity first, then choose counts for its estimand and detectable effect. Historical probability gates (such as 32-replicate M3 retests) apply only where their original protocol applies; do not copy them into every new test. In search comparisons, independent search runs are the unit; in migrating populations, the connected metapopulation is the unit.
   272	
   273	### Implementation slices and acceptance
   274	
   275	1. **Evidence fixture.** Reproduce an existing relevant result or construct a known-history physical fixture. Include a negative case that would fool the naive metric. Finish before adding search pressure.
   276	2. **Small CPU path.** Implement the minimum observer/assay and produce a manifest plus machine-readable evidence. Observers must not change physics; compare instrumented and plain twins.
   277	3. **Replay and backend qualification.** Test deterministic replay and the actual new invariants. GPU work needs GPU evidence, not a mocked adapter result. For CPU-only observers, explicitly state that limit.
   278	4. **Bounded pilot.** Benchmark runtime and calibration, choose the protocol, freeze held-out inputs, then collect scientific data. Do not tune on confirmation outcomes.
     1	// Deterministic baseline proposers (PLAN.md, section 4). A proposer takes only an envelope and returns a prediction (never
     2	// outcomes, never another case's task). Constants are frozen in protocol.json and passed in; nothing here reads a file or a clock.
     3	import { RULES, type Prediction as RulesPrediction } from "../propose.ts";
     4	import { PREDICTION_VERSION, taskKey, type CensusPoint, type Direction3, type PredictionEnvelope } from "./envelope.ts";
     5	import { donorAllowed, type Split } from "./splits.ts";
     6	import type { Prediction, Probs } from "./validate.ts";
     7	
     8	export interface Proposer {
     9	  id: string;
    10	  /** baseline: a rule a model has to beat; control: a deliberately uninformed run; oracle: knows the mechanism, a dev diagnostic only. */
    11	  role: "baseline" | "control" | "oracle";
    12	  propose(env: PredictionEnvelope): Prediction;
    13	}
    14	
    15	export interface ProposerParams {
    16	  fixedRule: { lead: number };
    17	  numericTrend: { window: number; minRelPerMille: number; lead: number; mid: number; low: number };
    18	  v0Rules: { lead: number };
    19	  mechanismRule: { lead: number; lowDoseQuanta: number; scarceNutrient: number };
    20	}
    21	
    22	export function predictionOf(env: PredictionEnvelope, probs: Probs | null, reason: string): Prediction {
    23	  const base = { version: PREDICTION_VERSION, caseId: env.caseId, envelopeHash: env.envelopeHash, task: env.task, reason };
    24	  return probs ? { ...base, probs, abstain: false } : { ...base, abstain: true };
    25	}
    26	
    27	/** `lead` per-mille on `dir`, the rest split evenly between the other two (the first, in order up/down/none, takes any odd remainder). */
    28	export function leadProbs(dir: Direction3, lead: number): Probs {
    29	  const rest = 1000 - lead;
    30	  const half = Math.floor(rest / 2);
    31	  const out: Probs = { up: 0, down: 0, none: 0 };
    32	  const others = (["up", "down", "none"] as const).filter((c) => c !== dir);
    33	  out[dir] = lead;
    34	  out[others[0]] = rest - half;
    35	  out[others[1]] = half;
    36	  return out;
    37	}
    38	
    39	/** Largest-remainder rounding of non-negative weights to per-mille summing to exactly 1000. */
    40	export function toPerMille(w: Record<Direction3, number>): Probs {
    41	  const tot = w.up + w.down + w.none;
    42	  if (!(tot > 0)) throw new Error("weights must have a positive sum");
    43	  const cls = ["up", "down", "none"] as const;
    44	  const raw = cls.map((c) => (w[c] * 1000) / tot);
    45	  const fl = raw.map(Math.floor);
    46	  let left = 1000 - fl.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0);
    47	  const order = raw.map((r, i) => ({ i, f: r - fl[i] })).sort((a, b) => b.f - a.f || a.i - b.i);
    48	  for (const o of order) {
    49	    if (left <= 0) break;
    50	    fl[o.i]++;
    51	    left--;
    52	  }
    53	  return { up: fl[0], down: fl[1], none: fl[2] };
    54	}
    55	
    56	export const fixedRule = (k: ProposerParams["fixedRule"]): Proposer => ({
    57	  id: "fixedRule",
    58	  role: "baseline",
    59	  propose(env) {
    60	    const kind = env.task.op.kind;
    61	    const dir: Direction3 = kind === "feed" ? "up" : kind === "lesion" || kind === "drain" ? "down" : "none";
    62	    return predictionOf(env, leadProbs(dir, k.lead), `fixed rule: ${kind} -> ${dir}`);
    63	  },
    64	});
    65	
    66	/** The census-only trend: slope of the primary readout over the last `window` samples, extrapolated over the horizon, as a share of the current level. Ignores frames and the task. */
    67	export function trendDirection(census: readonly CensusPoint[], horizon: number, k: ProposerParams["numericTrend"]): Direction3 {
    68	  if (census.length < k.window + 1) return "none";
    69	  const last = census[census.length - 1], first = census[census.length - 1 - k.window];
    70	  const span = last.step - first.step;
    71	  if (span <= 0) return "none";
    72	  // change over the horizon, per-mille of the current level, in integer arithmetic.
    73	  const delta = (last.mass - first.mass) * horizon * 1000;
    74	  const bar = k.minRelPerMille * Math.max(last.mass, 1) * span;
    75	  return delta >= bar ? "up" : delta <= -bar ? "down" : "none";
    76	}
    77	function trendProbs(dir: Direction3, k: ProposerParams["numericTrend"]): Probs {
    78	  if (dir === "none") return { up: k.low, down: k.low, none: 1000 - 2 * k.low };
    79	  const other: Direction3 = dir === "up" ? "down" : "up";
    80	  const out: Probs = { up: 0, down: 0, none: k.mid };
    81	  out[dir] = k.lead;
    82	  out[other] = 1000 - k.lead - k.mid;
    83	  return out;
    84	}
    85	export const numericTrend = (k: ProposerParams["numericTrend"]): Proposer => ({
    86	  id: "numericTrend",
    87	  role: "baseline",
    88	  propose(env) {
    89	    const d = trendDirection(env.census, env.task.horizon, k);
    90	    return predictionOf(env, trendProbs(d, k), `census trend -> ${d}`);
    91	  },
    92	});
    93	
    94	/**
    95	 * The shuffled-evidence control: the numeric-trend rule run on a donor family's census. The donor is assigned by the frozen
    96	 * derangement; a donor from the same family, the same history (a later window of it) or another split is refused (throws), so a
    97	 * control can never be handed the case's own future.
    98	 */
    99	export function shuffledEvidence(
   100	  k: ProposerParams["numericTrend"],
   101	  donors: Readonly<Record<string, string>>,
   102	  envelopeOf: (familyId: string) => PredictionEnvelope | undefined,
   103	  splitOf: (familyId: string) => Split | undefined,
   104	): Proposer {
   105	  return {
   106	    id: "shuffledEvidence",
   107	    role: "control",
   108	    propose(env) {
   109	      const donorFamily = donors[env.familyId];
   110	      const donor = donorFamily === undefined ? undefined : envelopeOf(donorFamily);
   111	      if (!donor) throw new Error(`no donor envelope for family ${env.familyId}`);
   112	      const ok = donorAllowed(env, donor, splitOf);
   113	      if (!ok.ok) throw new Error(`donor refused for ${env.caseId}: ${ok.reason}`);
   114	      const d = trendDirection(donor.census, env.task.horizon, k);
   115	      return predictionOf(env, trendProbs(d, k), `donor census trend -> ${d}`);
   116	    },
   117	  };
   118	}
   119	
   120	export const priorMajority = (devPrior: Probs): Proposer => ({
   121	  id: "priorMajority",
   122	  role: "baseline",
   123	  propose: (env) => predictionOf(env, { ...devPrior }, "dev-split class frequencies"),
   124	});
   125	export const alwaysNone = (): Proposer => ({ id: "alwaysNone", role: "baseline", propose: (env) => predictionOf(env, { up: 0, down: 0, none: 1000 }, "always none") });
   126	export const abstainAll = (): Proposer => ({ id: "abstainAll", role: "baseline", propose: (env) => predictionOf(env, null, "abstains on every case") });
   127	
   128	/** The v0 rule proposer's directional/replay prediction mapped into the schema: 600 on the stated direction, the remainder on none (explicit, versioned). */
   129	export const RULES_MAP_VERSION = 1;
   130	export function fromRulesProposal(env: PredictionEnvelope, pred: RulesPrediction, lead: number): Prediction {
   131	  const probs: Probs = pred.kind === "directional" && pred.direction ? { up: 0, down: 0, none: 1000 - lead, [pred.direction]: lead } as Probs : { up: 0, down: 0, none: 1000 };
   132	  return predictionOf(env, probs, `rules-v0 (map v${RULES_MAP_VERSION}): ${pred.kind}${pred.direction ? " " + pred.direction : ""}`);
   133	}
   134	export const v0Rules = (k: ProposerParams["v0Rules"]): Proposer => ({
   135	  id: "v0Rules",
   136	  role: "baseline",
   137	  propose(env) {
   138	    // The v0 rule table (tools/lib/propose.ts RULES): feed and drain rows from the nutrient-held signals, lesion from the standing-body signals, control otherwise.
   139	    const kind = env.task.op.kind;
   140	    const row = kind === "feed" ? RULES.livingCells.low : kind === "drain" ? RULES.livingCells.high : kind === "lesion" ? RULES.lineages.high : RULES.lineages.low;
   141	    const pred: RulesPrediction = row.action === "control" ? { kind: "replay", statement: "replays" } : { kind: "directional", statement: `${row.action}`, direction: row.direction };
   142	    return fromRulesProposal(env, pred, k.lead);
   143	  },
   144	});
   145	
   146	/** Positive control, a dev diagnostic only: knows the fixture mechanisms (lesion centre occupancy from the frames, dose against the nutrient in the field). Never a baseline to beat. */
   147	export const mechanismRule = (k: ProposerParams["mechanismRule"]): Proposer => ({
   148	  id: "mechanismRule",
   149	  role: "oracle",
   150	  propose(env) {
   151	    const op = env.task.op;
   152	    const last = env.frames[env.frames.length - 1];
   153	    const cen = env.census[env.census.length - 1];
   154	    const block = (x: number, y: number): number => {
   155	      const w = env.config.tileW * env.config.tilesX;
   156	      const bs = Math.floor(w / last.side);
   157	      return last.mass[Math.floor(y / bs) * last.side + Math.floor(x / bs)];
   158	    };
   159	    let dir: Direction3 = "none";
   160	    if (op.kind === "lesion") dir = block(op.x!, op.y!) > 0 ? "down" : "none";
   161	    else if (op.kind === "feed") dir = (op.r! * op.r! * 3 * op.amount!) >= k.lowDoseQuanta ? "up" : "none";
   162	    else if (op.kind === "drain") dir = cen.freeNutrient >= k.scarceNutrient ? "down" : "none";
   163	    return predictionOf(env, leadProbs(dir, k.lead), `mechanism: ${op.kind} -> ${dir}`);
   164	  },
   165	});
   166	
   167	export const caseKey = (env: PredictionEnvelope): string => taskKey(env.task);
     1	// Shared builders for the prediction-envelope tests: synthetic envelopes with no simulation, and a tiny protocol for the CPU fixtures.
     2	import { defaultConfig } from "@bl/schema";
     3	import { sealEnvelope, standardMenu, type CensusPoint, type Frame, type PredictionEnvelope, type TaskOp } from "../lib/prediction/envelope.ts";
     4	import { draftProtocol, type Protocol } from "../lib/prediction/run.ts";
     5	
     6	export function censusSeries(start: number, slope: number, n = 31, every = 10): CensusPoint[] {
     7	  return Array.from({ length: n }, (_, i) => ({ step: i * every, livingCells: 100, freeNutrient: 50, mass: start + slope * i * every }));
     8	}
     9	export function frames(): Frame[] {
    10	  const mass = new Array(64).fill(0);
    11	  mass[0] = 5;
    12	  return [{ step: 300, side: 8, mass }];
    13	}
    14	export function synthEnv(op: TaskOp, o: { familyId?: string; historyId?: string; caseId?: string; census?: CensusPoint[]; delta?: number; horizon?: number } = {}): PredictionEnvelope {
    15	  const cfg = defaultConfig({ tileW: 32, tileH: 32, kernelRadius: 4, seed: 1 });
    16	  return sealEnvelope({
    17	    version: 1,
    18	    caseId: o.caseId ?? "c-test",
    19	    historyId: o.historyId ?? `${o.familyId ?? "fA"}:s1`,
    20	    familyId: o.familyId ?? "fA",
    21	    ruleVersion: 1,
    22	    config: cfg,
    23	    census: o.census ?? censusSeries(1000, 0),
    24	    frames: frames(),
    25	    menu: standardMenu(cfg, 60, 4096, 6),
    26	    task: { subject: "world", op, delta: o.delta ?? 10, readout: "bodyMass", forkStep: 300, horizon: o.horizon ?? 200 },
    27	  });
    28	}
    29	export const LESION: TaskOp = { kind: "lesion", x: 1, y: 1, r: 4 };
    30	export const FEED: TaskOp = { kind: "feed", x: 1, y: 1, r: 4, amount: 64 };
    31	export const DRAIN: TaskOp = { kind: "drain", x: 1, y: 1, r: 4, amount: 64 };
    32	export const SHAM: TaskOp = { kind: "none" };
    33	
    34	/** A tiny protocol: 6 short families, 2 mechanisms' worth of work per family, short windows. minEffect and devPrior are set. */
    35	export function tinyProtocol(): Protocol {
    36	  const p = draftProtocol();
    37	  p.families = p.families.slice(0, 6);
    38	  delete p.cohorts;
    39	  p.nDev = 3;
    40	  p.nQualify = 2;
    41	  p.window = { forkStep: 40, delta: 5, horizon: 30, censusEvery: 10, frameSteps: [20, 40] };
    42	  p.mechanisms = p.mechanisms.filter((m) => ["feedOccupied", "lesionOccupied", "sham"].includes(m.id));
    43	  p.proposers.numericTrend.window = 2;
    44	  p.minEffect = 10;
    45	  p.devPrior = { up: 200, down: 300, none: 500 };
    46	  p.frozen = true;
    47	  p.donors = {};
    48	  return p;
    49	}
    50	
    51	import { buildOutputs, type Built } from "../lib/prediction/output.ts";
    52	import { generate, splitPlanOf } from "../lib/prediction/run.ts";
    53	import { donorsFor } from "../lib/prediction/splits.ts";
    54	
    55	let cached: { p: Protocol; built: Built } | undefined;
    56	/** A complete tiny pipeline (4 families, 3 mechanisms) built once per test file. */
    57	export function tinyPipeline(): { p: Protocol; built: Built } {
    58	  if (cached) return cached;
    59	  const p = tinyProtocol();
    60	  p.families = p.families.slice(0, 4);
    61	  p.nDev = 2;
    62	  p.nQualify = 1;
    63	  p.donors = donorsFor(splitPlanOf(p), p.salt);
    64	  const gen = generate(p, p.families.map((f) => f.id), true);
    65	  cached = { p, built: buildOutputs(p, gen) };
    66	  return cached;
    67	}

     1	// Static notebook view (PLAN.md, section 10): per case an observation block (census sparkline, frame thumbnails), a hypothesis
     2	// block (probabilities per proposer, with the free-text reason, escaped) and a measured-outcome block (the paired result), visibly
     3	// distinct. Failed predictions first; invalid or missing ones listed in their own section. No scripts, no external URLs, works from
     4	// file://. A pure function of its inputs.
     5	import { escapeHtml } from "../report-html/page.ts";
     6	import { labelOf } from "./fixtures.ts";
     7	import type { PredictionEnvelope } from "./envelope.ts";
     8	import type { ConfirmationDoc } from "./confirm.ts";
     9	import type { CaseRow, Comparison, PredictionLine, SplitTable } from "./run.ts";
    10	import { argmax, validatePredictionFile } from "./validate.ts";

    12	const esc = escapeHtml;
    13	const pct = (v: number | null): string => (v === null ? "n/a" : v.toFixed(3));
    14	
    15	function spark(env: PredictionEnvelope): string {
    16	  const pts = env.census.map((c) => c.mass);
    17	  const lo = Math.min(...pts), hi = Math.max(...pts), span = Math.max(1, hi - lo);
    18	  const w = 160, h = 40;
    19	  const d = pts.map((v, i) => `${((i / Math.max(1, pts.length - 1)) * w).toFixed(1)},${(h - 2 - ((v - lo) / span) * (h - 4)).toFixed(1)}`).join(" ");
    20	  return `<svg class="spark" viewBox="0 0 ${w} ${h}" role="img" aria-label="body mass over the observed steps ${env.census[0].step} to ${env.census[env.census.length - 1].step}"><polyline points="${d}" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.5"/></svg>`;
    21	}
    22	/** The last frame as four-level quantised paths (one path per level, empty blocks omitted), to keep the page small. */
    23	function thumb(f: PredictionEnvelope["frames"][number]): string {
    24	  const max = Math.max(1, ...f.mass), s = 6;
    25	  const paths: string[] = ["", "", "", ""];
    26	  f.mass.forEach((m, i) => {
    27	    if (m <= 0) return;
    28	    const lvl = Math.min(3, Math.floor((m / max) * 4));
    29	    paths[lvl] += `M${(i % f.side) * s} ${Math.floor(i / f.side) * s}h${s}v${s}h-${s}z`;
    30	  });
    31	  const body = paths.map((d, i) => (d ? `<path d="${d}" fill="currentColor" fill-opacity="${[0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1][i]}"/>` : "")).join("");
    32	  return `<figure class="thumb"><svg viewBox="0 0 ${f.side * s} ${f.side * s}" role="img" aria-label="mass by block at step ${f.step}">${body}</svg><figcaption>t=${f.step}</figcaption></figure>`;
    33	}
    34	function bar(p: { up: number; down: number; none: number }): string {
    35	  return `<span class="bar" aria-hidden="true"><i class="up" style="width:${p.up / 10}%"></i><i class="down" style="width:${p.down / 10}%"></i><i class="none" style="width:${p.none / 10}%"></i></span>`;
    36	}
    37	
    38	function tableHtml(t: SplitTable): string {
    39	  const head = ["proposer", "role", "n", "answered", "abstained", "invalid", "missing", "coverage", "Brier (imputed)", "Brier (answered)", "direction acc.", "balanced acc.", "acc. @ matched coverage", "valid non-null yield"];
    40	  const body = t.rows.map((r) => `<tr><th scope="row">${esc(r.id)}</th><td>${esc(r.role)}</td><td>${r.n}</td><td>${r.answered}</td><td>${r.abstained}</td><td>${r.invalid}</td><td>${r.missing}</td><td>${pct(r.coverage)}</td><td>${pct(r.brierImputed)}</td><td>${pct(r.brierAnswered)}</td><td>${pct(r.directionalAccuracy)}</td><td>${pct(r.balancedAccuracy)}</td><td>${pct(r.accuracyAtMatchedCoverage)}</td><td>${pct(r.validNonNullYield)}</td></tr>`).join("");
    41	  return `<h3>${esc(t.split)} (${t.nCases} cases, ${t.nFamilies} families; up ${t.classCounts.up}, down ${t.classCounts.down}, none ${t.classCounts.none})</h3><div class="scroll"><table><thead><tr>${head.map((h) => `<th scope="col">${esc(h)}</th>`).join("")}</tr></thead><tbody>${body}</tbody></table></div>`;
    42	}
    43	
    44	export interface ReportInput {
    45	  comparison: Comparison;
    46	  envelopes: readonly PredictionEnvelope[];
    47	  lines: readonly PredictionLine[];
    48	  rows: readonly CaseRow[];
    49	  minEffect: number;
    50	  proposerIds: readonly string[];
    51	  /** Whether a proposer was run on an envelope (the dev-only oracle is not shown, and not counted missing, on test cases). Default: always. */
    52	  eligible?: (proposerId: string, env: PredictionEnvelope) => boolean;
    53	  /** The sealed confirmation (I02b): adds the status banner, the reading and the failed predictions first. Absent for v0 reports, which stay byte-identical. */
    54	  confirmation?: ConfirmationDoc;
    55	}
    56	
    57	const ivText = (iv: { mean: number; lo: number; hi: number } | null): string => (iv ? `${iv.mean.toFixed(4)} [${iv.lo.toFixed(4)}, ${iv.hi.toFixed(4)}]` : "n/a");
    58	
    59	/** The confirmation section: banner, reading, the two primary comparisons, then numericTrend's failed predictions, then descriptive rows. */
    60	function confirmationHtml(d: ConfirmationDoc): string {
    61	  const r = d.reading;
    62	  const cmpRow = (name: string, c: ConfirmationDoc["reading"]["comparisons"]["vsPriorMajority"]): string => `<tr><th scope="row">${esc(d.primaryRule)} minus ${esc(c.comparator)} (${esc(name)})</th><td>${ivText(c.interval)}</td><td>${c.nFamilies}</td><td>${c.pairingComplete ? "yes" : "no"}</td></tr>`;
    63	  const failed = d.failedPredictions.length
    64	    ? `<ul>${d.failedPredictions.map((f) => `<li><a href="#${esc(f.caseId)}">${esc(f.caseId)}</a> family ${esc(f.familyId)}: ${f.status === "answered" ? `predicted ${esc(f.predicted ?? "?")}` : esc(f.status)}, measured ${esc(f.observed)}</li>`).join("")}</ul>`
    65	    : "<p>None.</p>";
    66	  const desc = d.descriptive.map((x) => `<tr><th scope="row">${esc(x.proposer)}</th><td>${x.n}</td><td>${x.answered}</td><td>${x.abstained}</td><td>${x.invalid}</td><td>${x.missing}</td><td>${pct(x.brierImputed)}</td><td>${pct(x.directionalAccuracy)}</td><td>${x.predictedClasses.up}/${x.predictedClasses.down}/${x.predictedClasses.none}</td><td>${pct(x.labelAgreementWithExpectation)}</td></tr>`).join("");
    67	  return `<section class="banner ${d.status === "CONFIRM" ? "confirm" : "rehearsal"}" aria-label="confirmation status"><p><b>Status: ${esc(d.status)}.</b> ${d.status === "CONFIRM" ? "A held-out confirmation on families fixed in a committed amendment before any outcome was computed." : "Not a held-out confirmation; no claim."}</p>
    68	<p><b>Reading: ${esc(r.label)}</b>${r.suffix ? ` (${esc(r.suffix)})` : ""}. ${esc(r.basis)}. ${r.testFamilies} test families, ${r.testCases} test cases, rule <code>${esc(d.primaryRule)}</code>.</p>${r.overrides.length ? `<ul>${r.overrides.map((o) => `<li>${esc(o)}</li>`).join("")}</ul>` : ""}</section>
    69	<h2>Primary comparisons (test split)</h2><p>Imputed multiclass Brier, per-family means, paired whole-family bootstrap, difference ${esc(d.primaryRule)} minus comparator (negative favours ${esc(d.primaryRule)}), mean [95% interval].</p>
    70	<div class="scroll"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">comparison</th><th scope="col">difference</th><th scope="col">families</th><th scope="col">pairing complete</th></tr></thead><tbody>${cmpRow("A", r.comparisons.vsShuffledEvidence)}${cmpRow("B", r.comparisons.vsPriorMajority)}</tbody></table></div>
    71	<h2>Failed predictions of ${esc(d.primaryRule)} (test split, ${d.failedPredictions.length})</h2>${failed}
    72	<h2>Descriptive rows (cannot produce a claim)</h2><div class="scroll"><table><thead><tr>${["proposer", "n", "answered", "abstained", "invalid", "missing", "Brier (imputed)", "direction acc.", "predicted up/down/none", "agrees with fixture expectation"].map((h) => `<th scope="col">${esc(h)}</th>`).join("")}</tr></thead><tbody>${desc}</tbody></table></div>`;
    73	}
    74	
    75	export function renderReport(inp: ReportInput): string {
    76	  const { comparison: c, envelopes, lines, rows, minEffect } = inp;
    77	  const eligible = inp.eligible ?? (() => true);
    78	  const proposerIds = inp.proposerIds;
    79	  const idsFor = (e: PredictionEnvelope): string[] => proposerIds.filter((id) => eligible(id, e));
    80	  const rowOf = new Map(rows.map((r) => [r.caseId, r]));
    81	  // Per-proposer validation (so invalid lines show up here as they do in the scorer).
    82	  const status = new Map<string, Map<string, "answered" | "abstained" | "invalid" | "missing">>();
    83	  const preds = new Map<string, Map<string, PredictionLine["prediction"] | null>>();
    84	  for (const id of proposerIds) {
    85	    const chk = validatePredictionFile(lines.filter((l) => l.proposer === id).map((l) => JSON.stringify(l.prediction)), envelopes);
    86	    const s = new Map<string, "answered" | "abstained" | "invalid" | "missing">(), pm = new Map<string, PredictionLine["prediction"] | null>();
    87	    for (const e of envelopes) {
    88	      const g = chk.byCase.get(e.caseId);
    89	      s.set(e.caseId, !g ? "missing" : !g.prediction ? "invalid" : g.prediction.abstain ? "abstained" : "answered");
    90	      pm.set(e.caseId, g?.prediction ?? null);
    91	    }
    92	    status.set(id, s);
    93	    preds.set(id, pm);
    94	  }
    95	  const envOf = new Map(envelopes.map((e) => [e.caseId, e]));
    96	  const failures = (caseId: string): number => {
    97	    const r = rowOf.get(caseId)!;
    98	    const o = labelOf(r.effect.bodyMass, minEffect);
    99	    let k = 0;
   100	    for (const id of idsFor(envOf.get(caseId)!)) {
   101	      const st = status.get(id)!.get(caseId)!;
   102	      const p = preds.get(id)!.get(caseId);
   103	      if (st === "invalid" || st === "missing" || (st === "answered" && p?.probs && argmax(p.probs) !== o)) k++;
   104	    }
   105	    return k;
   106	  };
   107	  // In a confirmation report the held-out test cases come before the dev cases, failed predictions first within each.
   108	  const testFirst = (e: PredictionEnvelope): number => (inp.confirmation && rowOf.get(e.caseId)!.split === "test" ? 0 : 1);
   109	  const ordered = [...envelopes].filter((e) => rowOf.has(e.caseId)).sort((a, b) => testFirst(a) - testFirst(b) || failures(b.caseId) - failures(a.caseId) || (a.caseId < b.caseId ? -1 : 1));
   110	  const caseHtml = (e: PredictionEnvelope): string => {
   111	    const r = rowOf.get(e.caseId)!;
   112	    const o = labelOf(r.effect.bodyMass, minEffect);
   113	    const hyp = idsFor(e)
   114	      .map((id) => {
   115	        const st = status.get(id)!.get(e.caseId)!;
   116	        const p = preds.get(id)!.get(e.caseId);
   117	        if (!p || st === "invalid" || st === "missing") return `<li class="bad"><b>${esc(id)}</b> ${esc(st)}</li>`;
   118	        if (p.abstain) return `<li><b>${esc(id)}</b> abstains <q>${esc(p.reason ?? "")}</q></li>`;
   119	        const a = argmax(p.probs!);
   120	        return `<li class="${a === o ? "ok" : "bad"}"><b>${esc(id)}</b> ${bar(p.probs!)} up ${p.probs!.up} / down ${p.probs!.down} / none ${p.probs!.none} per-mille, says ${esc(a)} <q>${esc(p.reason ?? "")}</q></li>`;
   121	      })
   122	      .join("");
   123	    const op = e.task.op;
   124	    const opText = op.kind === "none" ? "no operation (sham)" : `${op.kind} at (${op.x}, ${op.y}) radius ${op.r}${op.amount !== undefined ? `, amount ${op.amount}` : ""}`;
   125	    return `<article id="${esc(e.caseId)}" class="case"><h3>${esc(e.caseId)} <small>family ${esc(e.familyId)}, ${esc(r.split)}</small></h3>
   126	<section class="obs"><h4>Observation (steps up to ${e.task.forkStep})</h4>${spark(e)}<div class="thumbs">${thumb(e.frames[e.frames.length - 1])}</div></section>
   127	<section class="hyp"><h4>Hypotheses</h4><p>Question: ${esc(opText)} at step ${e.task.forkStep + e.task.delta}; direction of the ${esc(e.task.readout)} difference at step ${e.task.forkStep + e.task.horizon}.</p><ul>${hyp}</ul></section>
   128	<section class="out"><h4>Measured outcome</h4><p>treated ${r.treated}, control ${r.control}, effect ${r.effect.bodyMass} (minimum effect ${minEffect}) so <b>${esc(o)}</b>; fixture expectation ${esc(r.expected)}${o === r.expected ? "" : " (measured differs)"}.</p></section></article>`;
   129	  };
   130	  const bad = ordered.filter((e) => idsFor(e).some((id) => ["invalid", "missing"].includes(status.get(id)!.get(e.caseId)!)));
   131	  const css = `body{font:15px/1.5 system-ui,sans-serif;max-width:72rem;margin:0 auto;padding:1rem;color:#1d2328;background:#fbfaf6}table{border-collapse:collapse;font-size:13px}th,td{border:1px solid #cfc9bb;padding:.25rem .5rem;text-align:left}.scroll{overflow-x:auto}.case{border:1px solid #cfc9bb;border-radius:6px;margin:1rem 0;padding:.5rem 1rem;display:grid;gap:.5rem}.obs{background:#eef3f6;padding:.5rem;border-radius:4px;color:#27475a}.banner{border:2px solid #1d2328;border-radius:6px;padding:.25rem 1rem;margin:1rem 0}.banner.rehearsal{border-style:dashed}.hyp{background:#fbf3e3;padding:.5rem;border-radius:4px}.out{background:#e8f2e8;padding:.5rem;border-radius:4px}.spark{width:10rem;height:2.5rem}.thumbs{display:flex;gap:.5rem;flex-wrap:wrap}.thumb{margin:0}.thumb svg{width:3.5rem;height:3.5rem;background:#fff;border:1px solid #cfc9bb}.thumb figcaption{font-size:11px}ul{padding-left:1rem;margin:.25rem 0}li.ok b{color:#1f6b2a}li.bad b{color:#9b2c1c}q{color:#555}.bar{display:inline-flex;width:6rem;height:.6rem;vertical-align:middle;margin-right:.4rem;background:#ddd}.bar i{display:block;height:100%}.bar .up{background:#2a7a3a}.bar .down{background:#b23b2a}.bar .none{background:#8a8f94}small{color:#666;font-weight:normal}code{overflow-wrap:anywhere}`;
   132	  return `<!doctype html><html lang="en"><head><meta charset="utf-8"><meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width,initial-scale=1"><title>${inp.confirmation ? "Prediction envelope: confirmation notebook" : "Prediction envelope v0: notebook"}</title><style>${css}</style></head><body>
   133	<h1>${inp.confirmation ? "Prediction envelope: confirmation notebook" : "Prediction envelope v0: notebook"}</h1>
   134	<p>${esc(c.noModelStatement)}</p>
   135	<p>Protocol hash <code>${esc(c.protocolHash)}</code>. Strongest simple baseline on dev: <b>${esc(c.strongestBaselineOnDev)}</b>. Primary readout: body mass (B+P), minimum effect ${minEffect}. Failed predictions first.</p>
   136	<p>The mechanism oracle is a dev diagnostic: it is run on, and shown for, dev cases only.</p>
   137	${inp.confirmation ? confirmationHtml(inp.confirmation) : ""}<h2>Comparison</h2>${tableHtml(c.dev)}${tableHtml(c.test)}${tableHtml(c.testExcludingMeasurementLimited)}
   138	<h2>Invalid or missing predictions</h2>${bad.length ? `<ul>${bad.map((e) => `<li><a href="#${esc(e.caseId)}">${esc(e.caseId)}</a></li>`).join("")}</ul>` : "<p>None.</p>"}
   139	<h2>Cases</h2>${ordered.map(caseHtml).join("\n")}
   140	</body></html>
   141	`;
   142	}

Found **3 P1s and 2 P2s**.

1. **P1 — Missing families silently disappear from the confirmation.** [confirm.ts:171](/Users/nicholas/develop/browser-life-i02-confirm/tools/lib/prediction/confirm.ts:171), [predict.ts:178](/Users/nicholas/develop/browser-life-i02-confirm/tools/predict.ts:178)  
   Ledger membership comes from supplied outcomes/envelopes, rather than the frozen case inventory. I reproduced this with synthetic inputs: removing one family’s outcomes changed 16 families/96 cases to 15/90 while retaining **POSITIVE**, complete pairing, and no overrides. Analysis never verifies the incoming canonical manifest and subsequently replaces it, resealing the incomplete inputs. **Fix:** verify existing digests and exact protocol-derived case coverage before analysis; reject missing outcomes/envelopes. Add a test exercising `buildLedger()`, which the synthetic reading tests bypass.

2. **P1 — Analysis can label previously inspected v0 families as held-out confirmation.** [predict.ts:176](/Users/nicholas/develop/browser-life-i02-confirm/tools/predict.ts:176), [report-html.ts:67](/Users/nicholas/develop/browser-life-i02-confirm/tools/lib/prediction/report-html.ts:67)  
   `confirm-analysis` trusts `meta.status` without requiring amendment provenance. Default v0 `run --stage confirm` produces `CONFIRM` using its committed freeze, although EVIDENCE.md explicitly says those families were inspected. Analysis then renders “fixed in a committed amendment before any outcome was computed.” **Fix:** require and verify amended-run provenance, snapshot identity, and freeze identity before assigning confirmation status or displaying that claim.

3. **P1 — Smoke bypasses the protection against consuming reserved families.** [predict.ts:104](/Users/nicholas/develop/browser-life-i02-confirm/tools/predict.ts:104)  
   `run --stage smoke --protocol experiments/prediction-envelope/protocol.confirm.json --out /tmp/...` reaches `generate()` without amendment, freeze, or clean-tree checks. It can consume the fresh families before commitment; a subsequent confirm still claims held-out status. Thus REVIEW disposition #2 remains incompletely addressed. **Fix:** reject reserved confirmation identities in smoke, including renamed snapshots, and add a refusal test. I did **not** execute this command.

4. **P2 — Default v0 reproduction changes bytes.** [report-html.ts:131](/Users/nicholas/develop/browser-life-i02-confirm/tools/lib/prediction/report-html.ts:131)  
   Confirmation CSS is appended unconditionally, changing ordinary v0 `report.html` and therefore `canonical.sha256`, contrary to PLAN’s reproduction requirement and the byte-identical promise at line 53. An in-memory comparison against HEAD confirmed the added CSS alone causes the difference. **Fix:** emit it only when confirmation input exists; test legacy rendering against the preserved output.

5. **P2 — Successful runs record misleading source dirtiness.** [predict.ts:156](/Users/nicholas/develop/browser-life-i02-confirm/tools/predict.ts:156)  
   Git status is sampled after outputs are written. With the prescribed output directory inside `experiments/prediction-envelope`, those new artifacts make `meta.gitDirty` true despite the clean pre-run gate. **Fix:** capture source status before generation and output writes.

Deno checks passed. Recipe, freeze hash, disjointness, and split checks matched the amendment. No scoring/physics changes found; no confirmation families simulated or files edited.
